# Contract reference
Generated by `bun run contract:schemas` from the describe texts of the engine's schemas and its registry of rules: never edit it by hand.

A key is written as its path from its file's root, `<name>` standing for any name of a map; its anchor is the file and the path in lower case (`#screen-anchors-name-secret`), and a rule's is `rule-<id>`.

## Screen file

One screen of the application.

### `id`

<a id="screen-id"></a>
A screen name of the vocabulary: one or two kebab-case segments.

### `title`

<a id="screen-title"></a>
The title of the screen, for a reader.

### `description`

<a id="screen-description"></a>
What the screen is for: the task it serves and when it is shown. Not a list of its parts: each anchor has its own description. See the rule `description-purposes`. At most 200 characters.

### `frame`

<a id="screen-frame"></a>
The frame around every screen: it has no route of its own.

### `bindings`

<a id="screen-bindings"></a>
The routes of the screen: at least one unless the screen is the frame.

### `bindings.path`

<a id="screen-bindings-path"></a>
The route path: static segments, `$param`, `{-$param}` (optional) and `$` (splat), from `/`.

### `bindings.guard`

<a id="screen-bindings-guard"></a>
Where an account the route is not for is sent: a guest to sign-in (`authenticated`), or a signed-in account to the profile (`guest`); absent: nobody is sent.

### `bindings.roles`

<a id="screen-bindings-roles"></a>
The roles the route serves: it opens for an account that holds one, after the guard and before the search is read, and the not-found screen opens at the same address for any other.

### `bindings.params`

<a id="screen-bindings-params"></a>
The path parameters, as a flat JSON Schema.

### `bindings.params.type`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-type"></a>
Always "object".

### `bindings.params.properties`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties"></a>
The parameters by name.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name"></a>
One flat parameter.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.type`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-type"></a>
The primitive type of the parameter.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.enum`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-enum"></a>
The values the parameter takes.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.default`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-default"></a>
The value when the URL leaves it out.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.examples`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-examples"></a>
Fake values of the parameter, each one it accepts; the first fills the example link.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.minimum`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-minimum"></a>
The smallest value of a number or an integer.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.maximum`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-maximum"></a>
The largest value of a number or an integer.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.minLength`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-minlength"></a>
The fewest characters of a string.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.maxLength`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-maxlength"></a>
The most characters of a string.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.pattern`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-pattern"></a>
A regular expression (ECMA-262) every value of a string matches.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.description`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-description"></a>
What the route parameter means. Its type, bounds, default and examples say the rest. At most 200 characters.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.x-perch`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-x-perch"></a>
The controls of the page that set the parameter.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.x-perch.control`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-x-perch-control"></a>
The anchor of the control that sets the parameter.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.x-perch.values`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-x-perch-values"></a>
Per enum value, the control that chooses it.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-x-perch-values-name"></a>
How a page sets one enum value of the parameter.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>.control`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-x-perch-values-name-control"></a>
The anchor of the control that chooses this value.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>.controlValue`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-x-perch-values-name-controlvalue"></a>
The data-value of that control which chooses this value.

### `bindings.params.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>.note`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-properties-name-x-perch-values-name-note"></a>
Anything a reader needs to know about the value.

### `bindings.params.required`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-required"></a>
The parameters the route needs.

### `bindings.params.additionalProperties`

<a id="screen-bindings-params-additionalproperties"></a>
Always false: no other parameter.

### `bindings.search`

<a id="screen-bindings-search"></a>
The search parameters, as a flat JSON Schema. The one a platform action's mail links to this screen with is derived from version 4: a file never writes it. See the rule `mail-link-search`.

### `bindings.search.type`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-type"></a>
Always "object".

### `bindings.search.properties`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties"></a>
The parameters by name.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name"></a>
One flat parameter.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.type`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-type"></a>
The primitive type of the parameter.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.enum`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-enum"></a>
The values the parameter takes.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.default`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-default"></a>
The value when the URL leaves it out.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.examples`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-examples"></a>
Fake values of the parameter, each one it accepts; the first fills the example link.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.minimum`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-minimum"></a>
The smallest value of a number or an integer.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.maximum`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-maximum"></a>
The largest value of a number or an integer.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.minLength`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-minlength"></a>
The fewest characters of a string.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.maxLength`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-maxlength"></a>
The most characters of a string.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.pattern`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-pattern"></a>
A regular expression (ECMA-262) every value of a string matches.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.description`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-description"></a>
What the route parameter means. Its type, bounds, default and examples say the rest. At most 200 characters.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.x-perch`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-x-perch"></a>
The controls of the page that set the parameter, and whether it is the screen's key.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.x-perch.control`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-x-perch-control"></a>
The anchor of the control that sets the parameter.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.x-perch.values`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-x-perch-values"></a>
Per enum value, the control that chooses it.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-x-perch-values-name"></a>
How a page sets one enum value of the parameter.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>.control`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-x-perch-values-name-control"></a>
The anchor of the control that chooses this value.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>.controlValue`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-x-perch-values-name-controlvalue"></a>
The data-value of that control which chooses this value.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>.note`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-x-perch-values-name-note"></a>
Anything a reader needs to know about the value.

### `bindings.search.properties.<name>.x-perch.key`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-properties-name-x-perch-key"></a>
The screen's key: a missing or broken value fails with this code, from an action whose `{ search: <name> }` feeds a param referring to a value. One per route, no `default`. See the rule `address-key`.

### `bindings.search.required`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-required"></a>
The parameters the route needs.

### `bindings.search.additionalProperties`

<a id="screen-bindings-search-additionalproperties"></a>
Always false: no other parameter.

### `bindings.history`

<a id="screen-bindings-history"></a>
Whether opening the route adds a history entry or replaces the current one.

### `bindings.retain`

<a id="screen-bindings-retain"></a>
The search parameters carried across navigations.

### `flag`

<a id="screen-flag"></a>
The feature flag that shows the screen, or null for always.

### `entry`

<a id="screen-entry"></a>
The anchors that are present once the screen is open.

### `anchors`

<a id="screen-anchors"></a>
The declared anchors by name.

### `anchors.<name>`

<a id="screen-anchors-name"></a>
One declared anchor, as a screen file writes it.

### `anchors.<name>.kind`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-kind"></a>
The kind of the anchor; matches the end of its last segment.

### `anchors.<name>.description`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-description"></a>
What the element is for. Its kind, binding, codes, states, roles and flow say the rest; leave out what the engine says itself. See the rule `description-purposes`. At most 200 characters.

### `anchors.<name>.binding`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-binding"></a>
What the anchor does or shows: a field of the model, an action, a route, or (on a field that holds no secret) the search parameter it starts with. See the rules `anchor-binding` and `flow-effects`.

### `anchors.<name>.binding.model`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-binding-model"></a>
`entity.field`: a field of an entity of model.schema.json.

### `anchors.<name>.binding.action`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-binding-action"></a>
`entity.action` (an action of the entity x-perch) or `platform.<name>` (an action of platform.actions).

### `anchors.<name>.binding.params`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-binding-params"></a>
Per parameter of the action, the anchor whose value it takes, a constant, or a search parameter of the route.

### `anchors.<name>.binding.params.<name>`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-binding-params-name"></a>
An anchor (the control whose value it takes), a constant `{ "value": ... }`, or a search parameter of the screen's route `{ "search": "<name>" }`.

### `anchors.<name>.binding.params.<name>.value`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-binding-params-name-value"></a>
The constant: a string, a number or a boolean.

### `anchors.<name>.binding.params.<name>.search`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-binding-params-name-search"></a>
The name of a search parameter every route of the screen declares.

### `anchors.<name>.binding.route`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-binding-route"></a>
The path of a binding the anchor navigates to.

### `anchors.<name>.binding.search`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-binding-search"></a>
The name of a search parameter every route of the screen declares.

### `anchors.<name>.roles`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-roles"></a>
Who sees the element, among the roles its screen's bindings serve. Absent, it adds no limit: those above it and a link's derived roles hold. See the rules `anchor-roles`, `roles` and `link-roles`.

### `anchors.<name>.own`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-own"></a>
Shown to a member only on the entries the member owns: the anchor sits in an entry of a list, and shows on the entries the member owns alone. See the rule `own-entries`.

### `anchors.<name>.holdsInput`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-holdsinput"></a>
Whether typed text stays in the field until an action takes it or a `clear` empties it. Left out, it is derived: false where its own change alone applies it at once. See the rule `field-holds-input`.

### `anchors.<name>.marksState`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-marksstate"></a>
`false`: an `input`, `textarea` or `select` that holds input shows no state colour, its `data-state` still kept. Left out, it shows it. See the rules `state-colour` and `check-sources`.

### `anchors.<name>.secret`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-secret"></a>
A field that holds a secret (a password, its confirmation, a current password): it never comes from or goes into a URL, and leaves only in its action's request. See the rule `secret-field`.

### `anchors.<name>.with`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-with"></a>
The element renders only while every entry matches: an anchor while it renders, `{ <anchor>: [codes] }` while that anchor shows one of the codes. See the rule `with-without`.

### `anchors.<name>.with.<name>`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-with-name"></a>
A code of the vocabulary (data-code).

### `anchors.<name>.without`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-without"></a>
The element renders only while no entry matches: an anchor while it renders, `{ <anchor>: [codes] }` while that anchor shows one of the codes. See the rule `with-without`.

### `anchors.<name>.without.<name>`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-without-name"></a>
A code of the vocabulary (data-code).

### `anchors.<name>.codes`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-codes"></a>
`data-code` values on a `message`, `error`, `toast` or `dialog`: vocabulary or derived toast codes; tests assert them, not words. An error may leave them out. See the rule `error-codes-and-fields`.

### `anchors.<name>.ranked`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-ranked"></a>
A message whose codes are in rank order: of the codes raised before it shows, it shows the first. Only on a message of two codes or more, each listed once. See the rule `ranked-message`.

### `anchors.<name>.fields`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-fields"></a>
Each error code with its field or `form`. From version 3 written entries override the derived code by code; before, they replace them all. See the rules `error-codes-and-fields` and `error-placement`.

### `anchors.<name>.fields.<name>`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-fields-name"></a>
A field of the screen, by its anchor, or `form`, the form as a whole.

### `anchors.<name>.dataId`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-dataid"></a>
What the `data-id` of the element holds: the id of the entity it shows, on an `item` or a `row`. A `data-id` is data, not contract: no check reads its value. See the rule `attributes`.

### `anchors.<name>.dataValue`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-datavalue"></a>
It or its options carry a `data-value`, a choice's stable key, not its label. From version 4 the loader sets it on a select; a screen file omits it there. See the rules `attributes` and `kind-parts`.

### `anchors.<name>.states`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-states"></a>
The `data-state` values it takes: an app, container or row state. Not the field states of an `input`, `textarea` or `select`: `ids` lists those. See the rules `attributes` and `field-states-carried`.

### `anchors.<name>.derived`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-derived"></a>
The derived suffixes it renders; a screen file omits a paged list's parts and, from version 4, a kind's own: the loader lists them. See the rules `derived-parts`, `kind-parts` and `visibility-button`.

### `anchors.<name>.source`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-source"></a>
Where a list's, section's or table's entries come from, and when they are read; no other kind has one. See the rule `list-reads` for when a list reads and the rule `paged-list` for a paged one.

### `anchors.<name>.source.entity`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-source-entity"></a>
The entity of model.schema.json the anchor lists.

### `anchors.<name>.source.order`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-source-order"></a>
The field the entries are ordered by.

### `anchors.<name>.source.direction`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-source-direction"></a>
The order direction.

### `anchors.<name>.source.platform`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-source-platform"></a>
The platform action whose result the anchor lists.

### `anchors.<name>.source.params`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-source-params"></a>
Per parameter of the action, a search parameter of the route `{ "search": "<name>" }`, which the list rereads when it changes while it renders, or a constant; never a field. See the rule `list-reads`.

### `anchors.<name>.source.params.<name>`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-source-params-name"></a>
A constant the parameter always takes: `{ "value": true }`.

### `anchors.<name>.source.params.<name>.value`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-source-params-name-value"></a>
The constant: a string, a number or a boolean.

### `anchors.<name>.source.params.<name>.search`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-source-params-name-search"></a>
The name of a search parameter every route of the screen declares.

### `anchors.<name>.source.paged`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-source-paged"></a>
The list is a page of the action's entries: its page and size are the route's search parameters, and its parts are derived, never written. See the rule `paged-list`.

### `anchors.<name>.composite`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-composite"></a>
The platform composite the field opts into, declared once in `platform.yaml`; the loader derives its list and items. Only a secret `input` opts in. See the rule `composite-of-the-platform`.

### `anchors.<name>.confirms`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-confirms"></a>
Written on an unbound secret input: names the other secret input of its screen, one that opts into a composite, whose password it repeats. See the rule `password-pair`.

### `anchors.<name>.confirm`

<a id="screen-anchors-name-confirm"></a>
`false`: no question before its destructive action. Left out, a control bound to one asks if the frame has a confirmation; refused on others. See the rule `destructive-question`.

### `states`

<a id="screen-states"></a>
The container states of the screen, rows of the vocabulary's Container states table: what is visible in each, never what each means. See the rule `readiness`.

### `states.<name>`

<a id="screen-states-name"></a>
What is visible on the screen in this container state; the vocabulary says what the state means, and each anchor what it is for in a description of its own. At most 200 characters.

### `flow`

<a id="screen-flow"></a>
The flow of the screen: an XState v5 machine config in a shorter form (targets, named effects, regions by pattern or button) that the loader expands. See the rule `flow-shorter-forms`.

### `flow.id`

<a id="screen-flow-id"></a>
The id of the machine: one segment, which the app gives its machine and an id target `#<id>.<state>` names; absent, the screen id. See the rule `flow-states`.

### `flow.description`

<a id="screen-flow-description"></a>
What the flow does as a whole that no single state says: how its areas or its modes go together. Its states, events, guards and effects say the rest. At most 200 characters.

### `flow.initial`

<a id="screen-flow-initial"></a>
The state the screen opens in.

### `flow.meta`

<a id="screen-flow-meta"></a>
What the contract says of the flow beyond XState: its guards and the sets of anchors its states render.

### `flow.meta.guards`

<a id="screen-flow-meta-guards"></a>
The guards by name, each with what it holds.

### `flow.meta.guards.<name>`

<a id="screen-flow-meta-guards-name"></a>
What a guard holds: a sentence, or the failure codes it holds for.

### `flow.meta.guards.<name>.codes`

<a id="screen-flow-meta-guards-name-codes"></a>
The codes it holds for: the action failed with one of them.

### `flow.meta.renders`

<a id="screen-flow-meta-renders"></a>
Anchors that several states render, named once: a state that lists the name in its `meta.renders` renders those anchors. A set serves two states or more. See the rule `render-sets`.

### `flow.meta.renders.<name>`

<a id="screen-flow-meta-renders-name"></a>
The anchors of the set, which each state that lists it renders.

### `flow.states`

<a id="screen-flow-states"></a>
The states by name: plain states, and parallel states of regions.

### `flow.states.<name>`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name"></a>
One state of the flow.

### `flow.states.<name>.description`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-description"></a>
What a tester or a user tells apart in the state that its events, effects and `meta.renders` do not say. At most 200 characters.

### `flow.states.<name>.type`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-type"></a>
The flow ends in this state.

### `flow.states.<name>.meta`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-meta"></a>
What the contract says of the state beyond XState: its address and the anchors it renders.

### `flow.states.<name>.meta.address`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-meta-address"></a>
The route that reaches this state: a link or a reload restores it.

### `flow.states.<name>.meta.address.path`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-meta-address-path"></a>
The path of a binding of the screen.

### `flow.states.<name>.meta.address.search`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-meta-address-search"></a>
Search parameters of the binding, each a literal (`$id` for a minted id).

### `flow.states.<name>.meta.address.search.<name>`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-meta-address-search-name"></a>
A string, a number or a boolean.

### `flow.states.<name>.meta.renders`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-meta-renders"></a>
List anchors or render sets. A set lists all its anchors. An anchor renders where it or an ancestor is listed, or in all states if neither is ever listed. List a part's base. See the rule `renders`.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry"></a>
The effects on entering the state.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.type`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-type"></a>
The effect: an anchor shows a code.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.params`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-params"></a>
Which anchor, and the codes it may show.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.params.anchor`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-params-anchor"></a>
The anchor that shows the code.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.params.codes`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-params-codes"></a>
The codes it may show: its data-code is one of them.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.params.to`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-params-to"></a>
The path of the binding the app moves to.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.params.search`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-params-search"></a>
Search parameters of the binding, each a literal (`$id` for a minted id), `{ anchor }`, the value a field of the screen holds, or `{ item }`, the data-id of the entry whose control the user acted on.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.params.search.<name>`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-params-search-name"></a>
A string, a number or a boolean.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.params.search.<name>.anchor`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-params-search-name-anchor"></a>
A field of the screen, declared on it.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.params.search.<name>.item`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-params-search-name-item"></a>
An item or a row of the screen, with a `dataId`.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.show`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-show"></a>
The anchor that shows a code: one of the codes the anchor declares, in its order.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.show.<name>`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-show-name"></a>
A code of the vocabulary (data-code).

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.clear`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-clear"></a>
The field to empty, or the anchor that carries codes and shows none any more.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.read`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-read"></a>
The list, section or table of the flow's own screen whose source is a platform action: it reads that action again.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.navigate`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-navigate"></a>
The binding the app moves to: its path, or `{ to, search }`.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.navigate.to`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-navigate-to"></a>
The path of the binding the app moves to.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.navigate.search`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-navigate-search"></a>
Search parameters of the binding, each a literal (`$id` for a minted id), `{ anchor }`, the value a field of the screen holds, or `{ item }`, the data-id of the entry whose control the user acted on.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.navigate.search.<name>`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-navigate-search-name"></a>
A string, a number or a boolean.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.navigate.search.<name>.anchor`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-navigate-search-name-anchor"></a>
A field of the screen, declared on it.

### `flow.states.<name>.entry.navigate.search.<name>.item`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-entry-navigate-search-name-item"></a>
An item or a row of the screen, with a `dataId`.

### `flow.states.<name>.exit`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-exit"></a>
The effects on leaving the state.

### `flow.states.<name>.on`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-on"></a>
Per event, what follows: an anchor (the user acts on it), a platform event of platform.events, or a data event of a field the screen shows.

### `flow.states.<name>.on.<name>`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-on-name"></a>
One transition, or a list of guarded ones, the unguarded one last.

### `flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.target`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-on-name-target"></a>
The state the machine moves to; absent: it stays where it is.

### `flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.guard`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-on-name-guard"></a>
A guard: a camelCase name of the flow `meta.guards`.

### `flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.read`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-on-name-read"></a>
No read after this outcome: it changes nothing a list of this screen shows. On an invoke's `onDone`/`onError` branch, never beside a `read` or under `read: false`. See the rule `read-after-an-action`.

### `flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.actions`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-on-name-actions"></a>
The effects, in order.

### `flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.description`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-on-name-description"></a>
What follows that the target, the guard and the effects do not say: where a success leads, what a failure shows, what restarts what. At most 200 characters.

### `flow.states.<name>.invoke`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-invoke"></a>
The action the state runs while the machine is in it.

### `flow.states.<name>.invoke.src`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-invoke-src"></a>
The action the state runs, as a binding names it.

### `flow.states.<name>.invoke.input`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-invoke-input"></a>
Per parameter, the anchor whose value it takes or a constant.

### `flow.states.<name>.invoke.input.<name>`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-invoke-input-name"></a>
An anchor (the control whose value it takes), a constant `{ "value": ... }`, or a search parameter of the screen's route `{ "search": "<name>" }`.

### `flow.states.<name>.invoke.input.<name>.value`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-invoke-input-name-value"></a>
The constant: a string, a number or a boolean.

### `flow.states.<name>.invoke.input.<name>.search`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-invoke-input-name-search"></a>
The name of a search parameter every route of the screen declares.

### `flow.states.<name>.invoke.onDone`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-invoke-ondone"></a>
What follows when the action succeeds.

### `flow.states.<name>.invoke.onError`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-invoke-onerror"></a>
What follows when the action fails.

### `flow.states.<name>.invoke.read`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-invoke-read"></a>
Nothing reads after this action: it changes nothing a list of the screen shows. An invoke that says it and reads in one of its outcomes is refused.

### `flow.states.<name>.invoke.toast`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-invoke-toast"></a>
No toast says the success of this action: the flow shows its outcome itself, or it is visible by itself.

### `flow.states.<name>.states`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states"></a>
The regions written, by name: two or more once the loader adds the region of each button bound to one action that the flow handles nowhere.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name"></a>
A region written in full, by its pattern (`draft`), or as the region of a button (`by`).

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.pattern`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-pattern"></a>
A control that follows a stored value or holds what was typed, when two regions or more share it. Its states, `following` and `edited`, list no `meta.renders`. See the rule `flow-shorter-forms`.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.description`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-description"></a>
What area of the screen the region is. Its states and their transitions say how it behaves. At most 200 characters.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.input`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-input"></a>
The control typed into.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.stored`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-stored"></a>
The data event of the stored value it follows.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.guard`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-guard"></a>
The guard that holds when what was typed is the stored value, a guard of `meta.guards`.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.by`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-by"></a>
The screen's own button bound to one action, whose region this is: the loader writes `idle` and `running`; its `description`, `onDone` and `onError` go here. See the rule `button-region`.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.onDone`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-ondone"></a>
What follows success; absent or untargeted, `idle`. From version 5 the loader first clears a declared `<part>.error` unless the branch shows it; never write that clear. See the rule `area-request`.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.onError`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-onerror"></a>
What follows when the action fails; absent, back to `idle` with the error of the part (`<part>.error`) showing the codes of the failure; a transition without a target goes back to `idle` too.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.toast`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-toast"></a>
No toast says the success of this action: the flow shows its outcome itself, or it is visible by itself.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.read`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-read"></a>
Nothing reads after this action: it changes nothing a list of the screen shows. An invoke that says it and reads in one of its outcomes is refused.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.initial`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-initial"></a>
The state the region starts in.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states"></a>
The states of the region by name.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name"></a>
One state of a region: a plain state that does not end, has no address and no nested states.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.description`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-description"></a>
What a tester or a user tells apart in the state that its events, effects and `meta.renders` do not say. At most 200 characters.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.meta`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-meta"></a>
What the contract says of the state beyond XState: the anchors it renders.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.meta.renders`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-meta-renders"></a>
List anchors or render sets. A set lists all its anchors. An anchor renders where it or an ancestor is listed, or in all states if neither is ever listed. List a part's base. See the rule `renders`.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.entry`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-entry"></a>
The effects on entering the state.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.exit`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-exit"></a>
The effects on leaving the state.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.on`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-on"></a>
Per event, what follows: an anchor (the user acts on it), a platform event of platform.events, or a data event of a field the screen shows.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.on.<name>`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-on-name"></a>
One transition, or a list of guarded ones, the unguarded one last.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke"></a>
The action the state runs while the machine is in it.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.src`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-src"></a>
The action the state runs, as a binding names it.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.input`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-input"></a>
Per parameter, the anchor whose value it takes or a constant.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.input.<name>`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-input-name"></a>
An anchor (the control whose value it takes), a constant `{ "value": ... }`, or a search parameter of the screen's route `{ "search": "<name>" }`.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.input.<name>.value`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-input-name-value"></a>
The constant: a string, a number or a boolean.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.input.<name>.search`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-input-name-search"></a>
The name of a search parameter every route of the screen declares.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.onDone`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-ondone"></a>
What follows when the action succeeds.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.onError`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-onerror"></a>
What follows when the action fails.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.read`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-read"></a>
Nothing reads after this action: it changes nothing a list of the screen shows. An invoke that says it and reads in one of its outcomes is refused.

### `flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.toast`

<a id="screen-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-toast"></a>
No toast says the success of this action: the flow shows its outcome itself, or it is visible by itself.

### `wireframe`

<a id="screen-wireframe"></a>
A text sketch of the layout: the default state of the screen in Playwright's aria snapshot grammar.

## Platform file

The version of the contract format and what the platform offers every screen.

### `version`

<a id="platform-version"></a>
The version of the contract format the files are written in, said here alone from version 2.

### `defaults`

<a id="platform-defaults"></a>
What a value takes where it writes nothing of its own, said once for every value; from version 6. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `defaults.maxLength`

<a id="platform-defaults-maxlength"></a>
Default for writable non-enum strings: platform value, model field or constrained inline param (no `value`). Own limit wins; platform `maxLength: null` opts out. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `values`

<a id="platform-values"></a>
The values by name, each declared once where its policy lives (`email`): a parameter of an action takes one by `value: <name>`. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `values.<name>`

<a id="platform-values-name"></a>
A value declared once: its type and its constraints, which every parameter that refers to it by name takes. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `values.<name>.type`

<a id="platform-values-name-type"></a>
The primitive type of the value.

### `values.<name>.enum`

<a id="platform-values-name-enum"></a>
The values it takes, each a string or a number.

### `values.<name>.minLength`

<a id="platform-values-name-minlength"></a>
The fewest characters of a string.

### `values.<name>.maxLength`

<a id="platform-values-name-maxlength"></a>
The most characters of a string; from version 6 a string value that writes none, no `enum`, takes `defaults.maxLength`, and `null` takes no limit. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `values.<name>.pattern`

<a id="platform-values-name-pattern"></a>
A regular expression (ECMA-262) every value of a string matches.

### `values.<name>.format`

<a id="platform-values-name-format"></a>
A string value's format: `email`. Its editable field is `type=email` in a form without `noValidate`; an action run without submitting skips the browser's check. See the rule `email-format`.

### `values.<name>.minimum`

<a id="platform-values-name-minimum"></a>
The smallest value of a number or an integer.

### `values.<name>.maximum`

<a id="platform-values-name-maximum"></a>
The largest value of a number or an integer.

### `values.<name>.description`

<a id="platform-values-name-description"></a>
What the value holds, for every parameter that refers to it. At most 200 characters.

### `values.<name>.examples`

<a id="platform-values-name-examples"></a>
Fake values the value accepts, for a test or an example to use: any list.

### `actions`

<a id="platform-actions"></a>
The platform actions by name: `platform.<name>` in a binding.

### `actions.<name>`

<a id="platform-actions-name"></a>
One platform action.

### `actions.<name>.description`

<a id="platform-actions-name-description"></a>
What the platform action does. What holds once it succeeds is its `result`, and its parameters, codes, roles and mail say the rest. At most 200 characters.

### `actions.<name>.params`

<a id="platform-actions-name-params"></a>
The parameters by name.

### `actions.<name>.params.<name>`

<a id="platform-actions-name-params-name"></a>
One parameter of a platform action: it writes its `type` or the `value` it refers to, and may write the keywords of a value beside it, each only narrowing the value. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `actions.<name>.params.<name>.type`

<a id="platform-actions-name-params-name-type"></a>
The type of the value: string, boolean, number; left out where `value` gives it.

### `actions.<name>.params.<name>.value`

<a id="platform-actions-name-params-name-value"></a>
The value the parameter takes: a name of `platform.values` or a model field, `<entity>.<field>`, with its constraints; a keyword beside it may narrow them, never widen. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `actions.<name>.params.<name>.enum`

<a id="platform-actions-name-params-name-enum"></a>
The values it takes, each a string or a number.

### `actions.<name>.params.<name>.minLength`

<a id="platform-actions-name-params-name-minlength"></a>
The fewest characters of a string.

### `actions.<name>.params.<name>.maxLength`

<a id="platform-actions-name-params-name-maxlength"></a>
The most characters of a string.

### `actions.<name>.params.<name>.pattern`

<a id="platform-actions-name-params-name-pattern"></a>
A regular expression (ECMA-262) every value of a string matches.

### `actions.<name>.params.<name>.format`

<a id="platform-actions-name-params-name-format"></a>
A string value's format: `email`. Its editable field is `type=email` in a form without `noValidate`; an action run without submitting skips the browser's check. See the rule `email-format`.

### `actions.<name>.params.<name>.minimum`

<a id="platform-actions-name-params-name-minimum"></a>
The smallest value of a number or an integer.

### `actions.<name>.params.<name>.maximum`

<a id="platform-actions-name-params-name-maximum"></a>
The largest value of a number or an integer.

### `actions.<name>.params.<name>.optional`

<a id="platform-actions-name-params-name-optional"></a>
The action runs without the parameter: a field that passes it may stay empty.

### `actions.<name>.params.<name>.codes`

<a id="platform-actions-name-params-name-codes"></a>
The codes of the action whose failure concerns this parameter, each one of its `codes`, beyond those named after it (`<parameter>-<suffix>`). See the rule `attributed-codes`.

### `actions.<name>.params.<name>.description`

<a id="platform-actions-name-params-name-description"></a>
What the parameter is for. Its type or the value it refers to, and its constraints, are written beside it. At most 200 characters.

### `actions.<name>.result`

<a id="platform-actions-name-result"></a>
What holds once the action runs: one outcome, or a list of outcome lines, the plain success first. A refusal is its code's condition. See the rule `action-outcomes`.

### `actions.<name>.codes`

<a id="platform-actions-name-codes"></a>
The codes a failure of the action shows; from version 3 each that ends like the code of a field concerns a parameter. See the rules `action-outcomes` and `attributed-codes`.

### `actions.<name>.codes.<name>`

<a id="platform-actions-name-codes-name"></a>
When the action answers with the code, as the server judges it. A rule several actions keep is an invariant, named under the code with or instead of it. At most 200 characters.

### `actions.<name>.codes.<name>.invariant`

<a id="platform-actions-name-codes-name-invariant"></a>
The invariant, by its name under `platform.invariants`, or a list of two or more when several refuse with this code; each has this code as its `refuses`.

### `actions.<name>.codes.<name>.condition`

<a id="platform-actions-name-codes-name-condition"></a>
A cause of the code that is no invariant, beside the ones named: when else the action answers with it. At most 200 characters.

### `actions.<name>.roles`

<a id="platform-actions-name-roles"></a>
The roles that may run the action.

### `actions.<name>.paged`

<a id="platform-actions-name-paged"></a>
The action answers one page: number parameters `page`, from 1, and `size`, one of its sizes; the page in its result's order, with the total the other parameters match. See the rule `paged-list`.

### `actions.<name>.paged.sizes`

<a id="platform-actions-name-paged-sizes"></a>
The sizes a page may have, each once, the smallest first.

### `actions.<name>.paged.default`

<a id="platform-actions-name-paged-default"></a>
The size of a page the route does not name, a size.

### `actions.<name>.changes`

<a id="platform-actions-name-changes"></a>
Required: the list, section or table `source` actions its success changes in its context; never itself, nor a switch of organisation or account. `[]`: none. See the rule `read-after-an-action`.

### `actions.<name>.mail`

<a id="platform-actions-name-mail"></a>
The mail the action sends, when it sends one.

### `actions.<name>.mail.to`

<a id="platform-actions-name-mail-to"></a>
The parameter of the action whose value the mail goes to, or `account`: the email of the account the action acts on. See the rule `mail-recipients`.

### `actions.<name>.mail.language`

<a id="platform-actions-name-mail-language"></a>
Where the mail's language comes from: a language parameter, `account`, `organisation` or `recipient` (never last), or a list of them, the first that has one speaking. See the rule `mail-recipients`.

### `actions.<name>.mail.link`

<a id="platform-actions-name-mail-link"></a>
Where the mail's link leads.

### `actions.<name>.mail.link.screen`

<a id="platform-actions-name-mail-link-screen"></a>
The screen the link opens, at the path of its route.

### `actions.<name>.mail.link.search`

<a id="platform-actions-name-mail-link-search"></a>
The token's route search parameter. From version 4 the loader adds it to each route from the same-named platform value (`type`, no `format`); screen files omit it. See the rule `mail-link-search`.

### `actions.<name>.notices`

<a id="platform-actions-name-notices"></a>
The notices the action sends, when it sends any: mails that only tell, with no token, each by its name.

### `actions.<name>.notices.name`

<a id="platform-actions-name-notices-name"></a>
The name of the notice, unique within the action: `password-changed`; a scenario names it `[notice:<action>.<name>]`.

### `actions.<name>.notices.to`

<a id="platform-actions-name-notices-to"></a>
The parameter of the action whose value the notice goes to, or `account`: the email of the account the action acts on, as it was before the action. See the rule `mail-recipients`.

### `actions.<name>.notices.language`

<a id="platform-actions-name-notices-language"></a>
Where the mail's language comes from: a language parameter of the action, or `account` (the account the action acts on). See the rule `mail-recipients`.

### `actions.<name>.notices.link`

<a id="platform-actions-name-notices-link"></a>
The plain link of the notice, when it has one: a password changed links to the reset of a password, if it was not the account's owner.

### `actions.<name>.notices.link.screen`

<a id="platform-actions-name-notices-link-screen"></a>
The screen the link opens, at a route that needs no parameter: the link carries no token and no search.

### `invariants`

<a id="platform-invariants"></a>
The invariants by name: each a rule said once, named by the actions it limits under the code it refuses with. See the rule `action-outcomes`.

### `invariants.<name>`

<a id="platform-invariants-name"></a>
A rule several actions keep, said once: each names it under the code it refuses with, and the engine derives the actions it limits. See the rule `action-outcomes`.

### `invariants.<name>.rule`

<a id="platform-invariants-name-rule"></a>
The rule, said once for every action that names it under the code it refuses with. At most 200 characters.

### `invariants.<name>.refuses`

<a id="platform-invariants-name-refuses"></a>
The code an action refuses with when the rule would break: each action names the invariant under it.

### `invariants.<name>.values`

<a id="platform-invariants-name-values"></a>
The numbers the rule states, by camelCase name: a count or a duration; the rule writes each as `{name}`. See the rule `action-outcomes`.

### `invariants.<name>.values.<name>`

<a id="platform-invariants-name-values-name"></a>
A count: a limit, how many.

### `invariants.<name>.values.<name>.seconds`

<a id="platform-invariants-name-values-name-seconds"></a>
The duration in seconds.

### `invariants.<name>.values.<name>.minutes`

<a id="platform-invariants-name-values-name-minutes"></a>
The duration in minutes.

### `invariants.<name>.values.<name>.hours`

<a id="platform-invariants-name-values-name-hours"></a>
The duration in hours.

### `events`

<a id="platform-events"></a>
The events the platform raises, by name, each with what it means: `platform.<name>` as an event of a flow.

### `events.<name>`

<a id="platform-events-name"></a>
What the event means: when the platform raises it. What follows is said by the flows that handle it. At most 200 characters.

### `leaving`

<a id="platform-leaving"></a>
Actions (named as in a binding) that drop typed input, e.g. a sign-out. Ask first only when `unsavedInput.actions` is `question` and a field holds unsaved input. See the rule `leave-questions`.

### `toasts`

<a id="platform-toasts"></a>
The platform actions whose success toasts, each with its toast's code (`organisations.create: organisation-created`); an unlisted platform action never toasts. See the rule `success-toast`.

### `toasts.<name>`

<a id="platform-toasts-name"></a>
A code of the vocabulary (data-code).

### `composites`

<a id="platform-composites"></a>
The composites by name: a list with its items the platform declares once (`password-rules`), derived into every screen whose secret input writes `composite: <name>`.

### `composites.<name>`

<a id="platform-composites-name"></a>
A composite the platform declares once where its policy lives: a list with its items, derived into every screen whose field opts in with `composite: <name>`.

### `composites.<name>.description`

<a id="platform-composites-name-description"></a>
What the composite is for: the list it derives on a screen carries it, and each item has a description of its own. At most 200 characters.

### `composites.<name>.list`

<a id="platform-composites-name-list"></a>
The last segment of the list the composite derives on a screen, `<screen>.<list>`: a `list`, so it ends with `-list` (`password-rule-list`).

### `composites.<name>.itemState`

<a id="platform-composites-name-itemstate"></a>
The data-state each item takes once what it says holds: a row state of the vocabulary (`met`).

### `composites.<name>.items`

<a id="platform-composites-name-items"></a>
The items by name, each with its description: the screen gets `<screen>.<list>.<name>-item`, an `item` with the item state.

### `composites.<name>.items.<name>`

<a id="platform-composites-name-items-name"></a>
What the item of the composite's list says: every screen that opts in derives an item anchor with it as its description, and the composite's `itemState` marks it. At most 200 characters.

### `unsavedInput`

<a id="platform-unsavedinput"></a>
Every leave that would lose what the user typed and did not save, and how the app asks first: through the question, by the browser, never, or it cannot.

### `unsavedInput.question`

<a id="platform-unsavedinput-question"></a>
The reserved dialog that asks in the app whether to leave and lose what was typed: its code names what it asks, its confirm-button leaves, its cancel-button stays.

### `unsavedInput.screen`

<a id="platform-unsavedinput-screen"></a>
A change of screen the user starts: a link, a control whose flow moves to another screen (the loader marks it `leaves`), Back, Forward.

### `unsavedInput.actions`

<a id="platform-unsavedinput-actions"></a>
A control bound to an action of `leaving`, before the action runs.

### `unsavedInput.unload`

<a id="platform-unsavedinput-unload"></a>
A reload, closing the tab or the window, an address typed by hand: the browser's own prompt, drawn outside the page, so no anchor or code marks it.

### `unsavedInput.state`

<a id="platform-unsavedinput-state"></a>
A change of state inside one screen: a tab, a search parameter, a form opened or closed.

### `unsavedInput.flow`

<a id="platform-unsavedinput-flow"></a>
A navigation a flow makes after its action completed: the action took the input.

### `unsavedInput.elsewhere`

<a id="platform-unsavedinput-elsewhere"></a>
A leave nobody started here: another tab or device signed the account in or out, a database that will not open, a flow moving on a platform or data event. Nothing here can keep what was typed.

### `reserved`

<a id="platform-reserved"></a>
The anchors the shell owns; a feature never declares them.

### `status`

<a id="platform-status"></a>
The readiness line of the app.

### `status.anchor`

<a id="platform-status-anchor"></a>
The anchor of the readiness line of the app.

### `status.states`

<a id="platform-status-states"></a>
The app states it takes, in order.

## Activity file

An activity across screens: an XState v5 machine config whose states are visits.

### `id`

<a id="activity-id"></a>
The id of the activity, the name of its file: `session`.

### `title`

<a id="activity-title"></a>
The title of the activity, for a reader.

### `description`

<a id="activity-description"></a>
What the activity is for: the task it serves across its screens. Its states are the visits, and the screens do the work. At most 200 characters.

### `roles`

<a id="activity-roles"></a>
The roles that take part in the activity.

### `data`

<a id="activity-data"></a>
What the activity carries from screen to screen, by name.

### `data.<name>`

<a id="activity-data-name"></a>
One thing the activity carries, and the field of the model it is.

### `data.<name>.description`

<a id="activity-data-name-description"></a>
What the activity carries from screen to screen; `model` names the field of the model it is. At most 200 characters.

### `data.<name>.model`

<a id="activity-data-name-model"></a>
`entity.field`: a field of an entity of model.schema.json.

### `initial`

<a id="activity-initial"></a>
The state the activity starts in.

### `meta`

<a id="activity-meta"></a>
What the contract says of the machine beyond XState: its guards.

### `meta.guards`

<a id="activity-meta-guards"></a>
The guards by name, each with what it holds.

### `meta.guards.<name>`

<a id="activity-meta-guards-name"></a>
What the guard holds, in one sentence the app implements. At most 200 characters.

### `states`

<a id="activity-states"></a>
The states by name: the visits.

### `states.<name>`

<a id="activity-states-name"></a>
One state of the activity: a visit to a screen.

### `states.<name>.description`

<a id="activity-states-name-description"></a>
What the visit is for in the task. Its `meta` names the screen and the address, and the screen has a description of its own. At most 200 characters.

### `states.<name>.type`

<a id="activity-states-name-type"></a>
The activity ends in this state.

### `states.<name>.meta`

<a id="activity-states-name-meta"></a>
What the contract says of the state beyond XState: its screen and address.

### `states.<name>.meta.screen`

<a id="activity-states-name-meta-screen"></a>
The screen the state is a visit to: a screen id.

### `states.<name>.meta.address`

<a id="activity-states-name-meta-address"></a>
The route of the visit: the activity is where its address is.

### `states.<name>.meta.address.path`

<a id="activity-states-name-meta-address-path"></a>
The path of a binding of that screen.

### `states.<name>.on`

<a id="activity-states-name-on"></a>
Per anchor of the state's screen or of the frame, where acting on it leads.

### `states.<name>.on.<name>`

<a id="activity-states-name-on-name"></a>
One transition, or a list of guarded ones, the unguarded one last.

### `states.<name>.on.<name>.target`

<a id="activity-states-name-on-name-target"></a>
The state the activity moves to: the next visit.

### `states.<name>.on.<name>.guard`

<a id="activity-states-name-on-name-guard"></a>
A guard: a camelCase name of the flow `meta.guards`.

### `states.<name>.on.<name>.description`

<a id="activity-states-name-on-name-description"></a>
What acting on the anchor leads to that the target and the guard do not say; the flows of the screens do the work. At most 200 characters.

## Terms file

The project's language: its contexts, its terms and its roles.

### `contexts`

<a id="terms-contexts"></a>
The bounded contexts, by name: one while the product is one.

### `contexts.<name>.meaning`

<a id="terms-contexts-name-meaning"></a>
What the bounded context is: the part of the product whose terms mean one thing in it. At most 200 characters.

### `terms`

<a id="terms-terms"></a>
The platform's terms, by the one word the contract uses for each.

### `terms.<name>.meaning`

<a id="terms-terms-name-meaning"></a>
What the term means in the contract: the one word its texts use for it. Its synonyms are the words they never use for it, and `storage` names it where storage does otherwise. At most 200 characters.

### `terms.<name>.synonyms`

<a id="terms-terms-name-synonyms"></a>
The words the contract's texts and the emitted constants never use for it.

### `terms.<name>.storage`

<a id="terms-terms-name-storage"></a>
Where storage names it otherwise: a table, `identity`, or a table's field, `identity.role`.

### `roles`

<a id="terms-roles"></a>
The roles a device holds, by name: what `roles` of a binding, an anchor or an action list.

### `roles.<name>.meaning`

<a id="terms-roles-name-meaning"></a>
What holding the role means on a device: whose account is signed in and what applies to it. At most 200 characters.

### `roles.<name>.synonyms`

<a id="terms-roles-name-synonyms"></a>
The words the contract's texts and the emitted constants never use for it.

### `roles.<name>.storage`

<a id="terms-roles-name-storage"></a>
Where storage names it otherwise: a table, `identity`, or a table's field, `identity.role`.

## Model (x-perch)

One entity of the data model.

### `type`

<a id="model-type"></a>
Always "object".

### `description`

<a id="model-description"></a>
What the entity is. Its fields and actions have descriptions of their own, and `x-perch` says where it lives and who may change it. At most 200 characters.

### `x-perch`

<a id="model-x-perch"></a>
What the contract says about the entity beyond its shape.

### `x-perch.table`

<a id="model-x-perch-table"></a>
The table of the entity.

### `x-perch.region`

<a id="model-x-perch-region"></a>
Where the entity lives: the account private region or the organisation one.

### `x-perch.id`

<a id="model-x-perch-id"></a>
The id prefix of the entity, from the id registry.

### `x-perch.owner`

<a id="model-x-perch-owner"></a>
The field that names the owner of an entry.

### `x-perch.can`

<a id="model-x-perch-can"></a>
Per role, per operation: on any entry or only on its own.

### `x-perch.actions`

<a id="model-x-perch-actions"></a>
The actions on the entity by name.

### `x-perch.actions.<name>`

<a id="model-x-perch-actions-name"></a>
One action on the entity.

### `x-perch.actions.<name>.description`

<a id="model-x-perch-actions-name-description"></a>
What the action does. Its params say which fields it sets, and what it fails with is derived from them. At most 200 characters.

### `x-perch.actions.<name>.params`

<a id="model-x-perch-actions-name-params"></a>
Per parameter, the field it sets (`$id` for a new id).

### `x-perch.actions.<name>.changes`

<a id="model-x-perch-actions-name-changes"></a>
Required: the list, section or table `source` actions its success changes in its context; never what another account reads for itself. `[]`: none. See the rule `read-after-an-action`.

### `x-perch.unique`

<a id="model-x-perch-unique"></a>
The field sets that are unique.

### `x-perch.index`

<a id="model-x-perch-index"></a>
The field sets that are indexed.

### `properties`

<a id="model-properties"></a>
The fields by name.

### `properties.<name>`

<a id="model-properties-name"></a>
One field of the entity, a JSON Schema.

### `properties.<name>.type`

<a id="model-properties-name-type"></a>
The JSON Schema type of the field.

### `properties.<name>.description`

<a id="model-properties-name-description"></a>
What the field holds. Its type, its constraints and `x-perch` say the rest, and the failure codes they give are derived. At most 200 characters.

### `properties.<name>.enum`

<a id="model-properties-name-enum"></a>
The values the field takes.

### `properties.<name>.x-perch`

<a id="model-properties-name-x-perch"></a>
How the field is stored and merged.

### `properties.<name>.x-perch.readonly`

<a id="model-properties-name-x-perch-readonly"></a>
No action changes the field once it is created.

### `properties.<name>.x-perch.crdt`

<a id="model-properties-name-x-perch-crdt"></a>
How concurrent edits merge; `none` for last-writer-wins.

### `properties.<name>.x-perch.record`

<a id="model-properties-name-x-perch-record"></a>
The table a record link points to.

### `properties.<name>.x-perch.ref`

<a id="model-properties-name-x-perch-ref"></a>
The entity of the model the field refers to.

### `required`

<a id="model-required"></a>
The fields every entry has.

### `additionalProperties`

<a id="model-additionalproperties"></a>
Always false: no other field.

## Manifest (derived)

The UI manifest: the contract of the application for tests and agents.

### `$schema`

<a id="manifest-schema"></a>
The JSON Schema of the file: `./manifest.schema.json`.

### `manifestVersion`

<a id="manifest-manifestversion"></a>
The version of the manifest format.

### `generatedAt`

<a id="manifest-generatedat"></a>
When the manifest was built, in ISO 8601.

### `product`

<a id="manifest-product"></a>
The product the manifest describes.

### `product.name`

<a id="manifest-product-name"></a>
The product name.

### `product.version`

<a id="manifest-product-version"></a>
The version of the root package.json.

### `product.commit`

<a id="manifest-product-commit"></a>
The commit the manifest was built from, or null outside git.

### `contract`

<a id="manifest-contract"></a>
The vocabulary of the contract, with instructions for an agent.

### `contract.version`

<a id="manifest-contract-version"></a>
The version of the contract format the contract declares.

### `contract.defaults`

<a id="manifest-contract-defaults"></a>
From version 6, what a value takes where it writes nothing of its own; the platform's values, the model and every field's value here carry it already. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `contract.defaults.maxLength`

<a id="manifest-contract-defaults-maxlength"></a>
Default for writable non-enum strings: platform value, model field or constrained inline param (no `value`). Own limit wins; platform `maxLength: null` opts out. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `contract.idAttribute`

<a id="manifest-contract-idattribute"></a>
The attribute that carries an anchor.

### `contract.idPattern`

<a id="manifest-contract-idpattern"></a>
The regular expression every rendered anchor matches.

### `contract.kinds`

<a id="manifest-contract-kinds"></a>
The kinds of anchors.

### `contract.kinds.<name>`

<a id="manifest-contract-kinds-name"></a>
What an element of the kind is.

### `contract.derivedSuffixes`

<a id="manifest-contract-derivedsuffixes"></a>
The suffixes of derived anchors: `<anchor>.<suffix>`.

### `contract.derivedSuffixes.<name>`

<a id="manifest-contract-derivedsuffixes-name"></a>
The part of a composite the suffix marks.

### `contract.derivedParts`

<a id="manifest-contract-derivedparts"></a>
Each derived suffix with the part it marks: its kind, its meaning and, when the suffix defines them, its data-value values (a `visibility-button`: `show`, `hide`).

### `contract.derivedParts.<name>`

<a id="manifest-contract-derivedparts-name"></a>
The part a derived suffix marks.

### `contract.derivedParts.<name>.kind`

<a id="manifest-contract-derivedparts-name-kind"></a>
The kind of the part: the last word of its suffix.

### `contract.derivedParts.<name>.description`

<a id="manifest-contract-derivedparts-name-description"></a>
What the part is: the meaning of its suffix in the vocabulary.

### `contract.derivedParts.<name>.values`

<a id="manifest-contract-derivedparts-name-values"></a>
The data-value values the suffix itself defines, never rows of `choiceValues`.

### `contract.derivedParts.<name>.values.<name>`

<a id="manifest-contract-derivedparts-name-values-name"></a>
What the part says while it carries the value.

### `contract.attributes`

<a id="manifest-contract-attributes"></a>
The attributes an anchored element or the page carries.

### `contract.attributes.<name>`

<a id="manifest-contract-attributes-name"></a>
`<on>: <meaning>`: the elements that carry it, then what it holds.

### `contract.appStateAnchor`

<a id="manifest-contract-appstateanchor"></a>
The anchor of the readiness line of the app.

### `contract.appStates`

<a id="manifest-contract-appstates"></a>
The data-state values of the app.

### `contract.appStates.<name>`

<a id="manifest-contract-appstates-name"></a>
What the state means.

### `contract.readyValue`

<a id="manifest-contract-readyvalue"></a>
The app state in which a test may start.

### `contract.containerStates`

<a id="manifest-contract-containerstates"></a>
The data-state values of a list, a section or a page status.

### `contract.containerStates.<name>`

<a id="manifest-contract-containerstates-name"></a>
What the state means.

### `contract.rowStates`

<a id="manifest-contract-rowstates"></a>
The data-state values of an item.

### `contract.rowStates.<name>`

<a id="manifest-contract-rowstates-name"></a>
`<where>: <meaning>`: the anchor that takes it, then what it means.

### `contract.codes`

<a id="manifest-contract-codes"></a>
The data-code values: what a message, error, toast or dialog says.

### `contract.codes.<name>`

<a id="manifest-contract-codes-name"></a>
`<where>: <meaning>`: the anchors that carry it, then what it means.

### `contract.choiceValues`

<a id="manifest-contract-choicevalues"></a>
The data-value values by anchor; a value that starts with `^` is a pattern of values.

### `contract.choiceValues.<name>`

<a id="manifest-contract-choicevalues-name"></a>
The values the anchor carries.

### `contract.choiceValues.<name>.<name>`

<a id="manifest-contract-choicevalues-name-name"></a>
What the value chooses.

### `contract.roles`

<a id="manifest-contract-roles"></a>
The roles a device acts as.

### `contract.roles.<name>`

<a id="manifest-contract-roles-name"></a>
Who acts in the role.

### `contract.screenNames`

<a id="manifest-contract-screennames"></a>
The screen names an anchor starts with.

### `contract.screenNames.<name>`

<a id="manifest-contract-screennames-name"></a>
The screen and its route.

### `contract.reserved`

<a id="manifest-contract-reserved"></a>
The anchors the shell owns; a feature never declares them.

### `contract.composites`

<a id="manifest-contract-composites"></a>
The composites the platform declares once, derived into the screens whose field opts in; the declaration itself is under `platform.composites`.

### `contract.composites.<name>`

<a id="manifest-contract-composites-name"></a>
What the composite is for.

### `contract.fieldStates`

<a id="manifest-contract-fieldstates"></a>
The data-state values of a field that holds input; `unsaved` and `saved`.

### `contract.fieldStates.<name>`

<a id="manifest-contract-fieldstates-name"></a>
What the state of a field means.

### `contract.pageHelper`

<a id="manifest-contract-pagehelper"></a>
Where development and e2e builds install the page helper on `window`.

### `contract.instructions`

<a id="manifest-contract-instructions"></a>
Prose instructions for an agent that drives the application.

### `contract.instructions.readiness`

<a id="manifest-contract-instructions-readiness"></a>
When a page is ready for a test to act on it.

### `contract.instructions.language`

<a id="manifest-contract-instructions-language"></a>
Which language the page speaks, and what to assert.

### `contract.instructions.session`

<a id="manifest-contract-instructions-session"></a>
How a scenario starts and makes its accounts.

### `contract.instructions.screenMap`

<a id="manifest-contract-instructions-screenmap"></a>
How to read `screens` and the fields of an anchor.

### `contract.instructions.bindings`

<a id="manifest-contract-instructions-bindings"></a>
Where each kind of binding points.

### `contract.instructions.model`

<a id="manifest-contract-instructions-model"></a>
How to read `model` and its `x-perch` keyword.

### `contract.instructions.notices`

<a id="manifest-contract-instructions-notices"></a>
How to find the notice an action raises.

### `contract.instructions.activities`

<a id="manifest-contract-instructions-activities"></a>
How to read `activities`, the machines of visits to screens.

### `contract.instructions.scenarios`

<a id="manifest-contract-instructions-scenarios"></a>
How to read `scenarios` and their bracket references.

### `contract.instructions.gaps`

<a id="manifest-contract-instructions-gaps"></a>
What to do when the contract cannot say what a build needs.

### `terms`

<a id="manifest-terms"></a>
`contract/domain/terms.yaml`, verbatim, when the contract writes it: the contexts, the platform's terms and the roles. See the rule `terms`.

### `terms.contexts`

<a id="manifest-terms-contexts"></a>
The bounded contexts, by name: one while the product is one.

### `terms.contexts.<name>.meaning`

<a id="manifest-terms-contexts-name-meaning"></a>
What the bounded context is: the part of the product whose terms mean one thing in it. At most 200 characters.

### `terms.terms`

<a id="manifest-terms-terms"></a>
The platform's terms, by the one word the contract uses for each.

### `terms.terms.<name>.meaning`

<a id="manifest-terms-terms-name-meaning"></a>
What the term means in the contract: the one word its texts use for it. Its synonyms are the words they never use for it, and `storage` names it where storage does otherwise. At most 200 characters.

### `terms.terms.<name>.synonyms`

<a id="manifest-terms-terms-name-synonyms"></a>
The words the contract's texts and the emitted constants never use for it.

### `terms.terms.<name>.storage`

<a id="manifest-terms-terms-name-storage"></a>
Where storage names it otherwise: a table, `identity`, or a table's field, `identity.role`.

### `terms.roles`

<a id="manifest-terms-roles"></a>
The roles a device holds, by name: what `roles` of a binding, an anchor or an action list.

### `terms.roles.<name>.meaning`

<a id="manifest-terms-roles-name-meaning"></a>
What holding the role means on a device: whose account is signed in and what applies to it. At most 200 characters.

### `terms.roles.<name>.synonyms`

<a id="manifest-terms-roles-name-synonyms"></a>
The words the contract's texts and the emitted constants never use for it.

### `terms.roles.<name>.storage`

<a id="manifest-terms-roles-name-storage"></a>
Where storage names it otherwise: a table, `identity`, or a table's field, `identity.role`.

### `screens`

<a id="manifest-screens"></a>
The screens of `contract/domain/screens/`, verbatim: routes with their example links, anchors, flows.

### `screens.id`

<a id="manifest-screens-id"></a>
A screen name of the vocabulary: one or two kebab-case segments.

### `screens.title`

<a id="manifest-screens-title"></a>
The title of the screen, for a reader.

### `screens.description`

<a id="manifest-screens-description"></a>
What the screen is for: the task it serves and when it is shown. Not a list of its parts: each anchor has its own description. See the rule `description-purposes`. At most 200 characters.

### `screens.frame`

<a id="manifest-screens-frame"></a>
The frame around every screen: it has no route of its own.

### `screens.bindings`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings"></a>
The routes of the screen, each with its example link.

### `screens.bindings.path`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-path"></a>
The route path: static segments, `$param`, `{-$param}` (optional) and `$` (splat), from `/`.

### `screens.bindings.guard`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-guard"></a>
Where an account the route is not for is sent: a guest to sign-in (`authenticated`), or a signed-in account to the profile (`guest`); absent: nobody is sent.

### `screens.bindings.roles`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-roles"></a>
The roles the route serves: it opens for an account that holds one, after the guard and before the search is read, and the not-found screen opens at the same address for any other.

### `screens.bindings.params`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params"></a>
The path parameters, as a flat JSON Schema.

### `screens.bindings.params.type`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-type"></a>
Always "object".

### `screens.bindings.params.properties`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties"></a>
The parameters by name.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name"></a>
One flat parameter.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.type`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-type"></a>
The primitive type of the parameter.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.enum`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-enum"></a>
The values the parameter takes.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.default`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-default"></a>
The value when the URL leaves it out.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.examples`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-examples"></a>
Fake values of the parameter, each one it accepts; the first fills the example link.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.minimum`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-minimum"></a>
The smallest value of a number or an integer.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.maximum`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-maximum"></a>
The largest value of a number or an integer.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.minLength`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-minlength"></a>
The fewest characters of a string.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.maxLength`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-maxlength"></a>
The most characters of a string.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.pattern`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-pattern"></a>
A regular expression (ECMA-262) every value of a string matches.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.description`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-description"></a>
What the route parameter means. Its type, bounds, default and examples say the rest. At most 200 characters.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.x-perch`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-x-perch"></a>
The controls of the page that set the parameter.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.x-perch.control`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-x-perch-control"></a>
The anchor of the control that sets the parameter.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.x-perch.values`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-x-perch-values"></a>
Per enum value, the control that chooses it.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-x-perch-values-name"></a>
How a page sets one enum value of the parameter.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>.control`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-x-perch-values-name-control"></a>
The anchor of the control that chooses this value.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>.controlValue`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-x-perch-values-name-controlvalue"></a>
The data-value of that control which chooses this value.

### `screens.bindings.params.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>.note`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-properties-name-x-perch-values-name-note"></a>
Anything a reader needs to know about the value.

### `screens.bindings.params.required`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-required"></a>
The parameters the route needs.

### `screens.bindings.params.additionalProperties`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-params-additionalproperties"></a>
Always false: no other parameter.

### `screens.bindings.search`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search"></a>
The search parameters, as a flat JSON Schema. The one a platform action's mail links to this screen with is derived from version 4: a file never writes it. See the rule `mail-link-search`.

### `screens.bindings.search.type`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-type"></a>
Always "object".

### `screens.bindings.search.properties`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties"></a>
The parameters by name.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name"></a>
One flat parameter.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.type`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-type"></a>
The primitive type of the parameter.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.enum`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-enum"></a>
The values the parameter takes.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.default`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-default"></a>
The value when the URL leaves it out.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.examples`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-examples"></a>
Fake values of the parameter, each one it accepts; the first fills the example link.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.minimum`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-minimum"></a>
The smallest value of a number or an integer.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.maximum`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-maximum"></a>
The largest value of a number or an integer.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.minLength`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-minlength"></a>
The fewest characters of a string.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.maxLength`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-maxlength"></a>
The most characters of a string.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.pattern`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-pattern"></a>
A regular expression (ECMA-262) every value of a string matches.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.description`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-description"></a>
What the route parameter means. Its type, bounds, default and examples say the rest. At most 200 characters.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.x-perch`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-x-perch"></a>
The controls of the page that set the parameter, and whether it is the screen's key.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.x-perch.control`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-x-perch-control"></a>
The anchor of the control that sets the parameter.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.x-perch.values`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-x-perch-values"></a>
Per enum value, the control that chooses it.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-x-perch-values-name"></a>
How a page sets one enum value of the parameter.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>.control`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-x-perch-values-name-control"></a>
The anchor of the control that chooses this value.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>.controlValue`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-x-perch-values-name-controlvalue"></a>
The data-value of that control which chooses this value.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>.note`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-x-perch-values-name-note"></a>
Anything a reader needs to know about the value.

### `screens.bindings.search.properties.<name>.x-perch.key`
