<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-properties-name-x-perch-key"></a>
The screen's key: a missing or broken value fails with this code, from an action whose `{ search: <name> }` feeds a param referring to a value. One per route, no `default`. See the rule `address-key`.

### `screens.bindings.search.required`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-required"></a>
The parameters the route needs.

### `screens.bindings.search.additionalProperties`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-search-additionalproperties"></a>
Always false: no other parameter.

### `screens.bindings.history`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-history"></a>
Whether opening the route adds a history entry or replaces the current one.

### `screens.bindings.retain`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-retain"></a>
The search parameters carried across navigations.

### `screens.bindings.example`

<a id="manifest-screens-bindings-example"></a>
A deep link to the route with fake data, as the router writes it: each path parameter at its first example, every search key at its default (else its first enum value or example).

### `screens.flag`

<a id="manifest-screens-flag"></a>
The feature flag that shows the screen, or null for always.

### `screens.entry`

<a id="manifest-screens-entry"></a>
The anchors that are present once the screen is open.

### `screens.anchors`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors"></a>
The declared anchors by name.

### `screens.anchors.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name"></a>
One declared anchor.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.kind`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-kind"></a>
The kind of the anchor; matches the end of its last segment.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.description`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-description"></a>
What the element is for. Its kind, binding, codes, states, roles and flow say the rest; leave out what the engine says itself. See the rule `description-purposes`. At most 200 characters.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.binding`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-binding"></a>
What the anchor does or shows: a field of the model, an action, a route, or (on a field that holds no secret) the search parameter it starts with. See the rules `anchor-binding` and `flow-effects`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.binding.model`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-binding-model"></a>
`entity.field`: a field of an entity of model.schema.json.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.binding.action`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-binding-action"></a>
`entity.action` (an action of the entity x-perch) or `platform.<name>` (an action of platform.actions).

### `screens.anchors.<name>.binding.params`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-binding-params"></a>
Per parameter of the action, the anchor whose value it takes, a constant, or a search parameter of the route.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.binding.params.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-binding-params-name"></a>
An anchor (the control whose value it takes), a constant `{ "value": ... }`, or a search parameter of the screen's route `{ "search": "<name>" }`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.binding.params.<name>.value`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-binding-params-name-value"></a>
The constant: a string, a number or a boolean.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.binding.params.<name>.search`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-binding-params-name-search"></a>
The name of a search parameter every route of the screen declares.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.binding.route`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-binding-route"></a>
The path of a binding the anchor navigates to.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.binding.search`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-binding-search"></a>
The name of a search parameter every route of the screen declares.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.roles`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-roles"></a>
Who sees the element, among the roles its screen's bindings serve. Absent, it adds no limit: those above it and a link's derived roles hold. See the rules `anchor-roles`, `roles` and `link-roles`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.own`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-own"></a>
Shown to a member only on the entries the member owns: the anchor sits in an entry of a list, and shows on the entries the member owns alone. See the rule `own-entries`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.holdsInput`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-holdsinput"></a>
True when what is typed stays in the field until an action takes it or a flow clears it, so a leave with it typed loses it; false when it holds none. See the rule `field-holds-input`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.marksState`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-marksstate"></a>
`false`: an `input`, `textarea` or `select` that holds input shows no state colour, its `data-state` still kept. Left out, it shows it. See the rules `state-colour` and `check-sources`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.secret`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-secret"></a>
A field that holds a secret (a password, its confirmation, a current password): it never comes from or goes into a URL, and leaves only in its action's request. See the rule `secret-field`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.with`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-with"></a>
The element renders only while every entry matches: an anchor while it renders, `{ <anchor>: [codes] }` while that anchor shows one of the codes. See the rule `with-without`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.with.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-with-name"></a>
A code of the vocabulary (data-code).

### `screens.anchors.<name>.without`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-without"></a>
The element renders only while no entry matches: an anchor while it renders, `{ <anchor>: [codes] }` while that anchor shows one of the codes. See the rule `with-without`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.without.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-without-name"></a>
A code of the vocabulary (data-code).

### `screens.anchors.<name>.codes`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-codes"></a>
The data-code values the element carries. On an `error`, `failed` shows for a failure whose code it does not list, and for one with no code.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.ranked`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-ranked"></a>
A message whose codes are in rank order: of the codes raised before it shows, it shows the first. Only on a message of two codes or more, each listed once. See the rule `ranked-message`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.fields`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-fields"></a>
An error's codes, each with the field it concerns, which it shows under and marks invalid, or `form`; a code left out concerns the form. See the rules `error-placement` and `error-codes-and-fields`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.fields.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-fields-name"></a>
A field of the screen, by its anchor, or `form`, the form as a whole.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.dataId`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-dataid"></a>
What the `data-id` of the element holds: the id of the entity it shows, on an `item` or a `row`. A `data-id` is data, not contract: no check reads its value. See the rule `attributes`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.dataValue`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-datavalue"></a>
It or its options carry a `data-value`, a choice's stable key, not its label. From version 4 the loader sets it on a select; a screen file omits it there. See the rules `attributes` and `kind-parts`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.states`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-states"></a>
The `data-state` values it takes: an app, container or row state. Not the field states of an `input`, `textarea` or `select`: `ids` lists those. See the rules `attributes` and `field-states-carried`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.derived`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-derived"></a>
The derived suffixes it renders; a screen file omits a paged list's parts and, from version 4, a kind's own: the loader lists them. See the rules `derived-parts`, `kind-parts` and `visibility-button`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.source`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-source"></a>
Where a list's, section's or table's entries come from, and when they are read; no other kind has one. See the rule `list-reads` for when a list reads and the rule `paged-list` for a paged one.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.source.entity`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-source-entity"></a>
The entity of model.schema.json the anchor lists.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.source.order`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-source-order"></a>
The field the entries are ordered by.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.source.direction`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-source-direction"></a>
The order direction.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.source.platform`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-source-platform"></a>
The platform action whose result the anchor lists.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.source.params`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-source-params"></a>
Per parameter of the action, a search parameter of the route `{ "search": "<name>" }`, which the list rereads when it changes while it renders, or a constant; never a field. See the rule `list-reads`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.source.params.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-source-params-name"></a>
A constant the parameter always takes: `{ "value": true }`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.source.params.<name>.value`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-source-params-name-value"></a>
The constant: a string, a number or a boolean.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.source.params.<name>.search`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-source-params-name-search"></a>
The name of a search parameter every route of the screen declares.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.source.paged`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-source-paged"></a>
The list is a page of the action's entries: its page and size are the route's search parameters, and its parts are derived, never written. See the rule `paged-list`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.asks`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-asks"></a>
Derived: the control asks the frame's confirmation before its destructive action runs. A screen file never writes it. See the rule `destructive-question`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.asks.dialog`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-asks-dialog"></a>
The frame's confirmation that asks.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.asks.code`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-asks-code"></a>
The code it asks with: what the action destroys.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.leaves`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-leaves"></a>
Derived: the control's event moves its flow to another screen, so it asks the frame's leave question first, as a link does.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.leaves.dialog`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-leaves-dialog"></a>
The frame's dialog that asks whether to leave and lose what was typed.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.leaves.loses`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-leaves-loses"></a>
The fields of its screen that hold input and that the move does not carry in its search: it asks while one of them holds unsaved input.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.composite`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-composite"></a>
The platform composite the field opts into, declared once in `platform.yaml`; the loader derives its list and items. Only a secret `input` opts in. See the rule `composite-of-the-platform`.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.derivedFrom`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-derivedfrom"></a>
Derived: the anchor is the list, or one item, of a platform composite a field of the screen opted into; a screen file never writes it.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.derivedFrom.composite`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-derivedfrom-composite"></a>
The composite that derives the anchor.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.derivedFrom.item`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-derivedfrom-item"></a>
The item of the composite the anchor stands for; absent: the list.

### `screens.anchors.<name>.confirms`

<a id="manifest-screens-anchors-name-confirms"></a>
Written on an unbound secret input: names the other secret input of its screen, one that opts into a composite, whose password it repeats. See the rule `password-pair`.

### `screens.states`

<a id="manifest-screens-states"></a>
The container states of the screen, rows of the vocabulary's Container states table: what is visible in each, never what each means. See the rule `readiness`.

### `screens.states.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-states-name"></a>
What is visible on the screen in this container state; the vocabulary says what the state means, and each anchor what it is for in a description of its own. At most 200 characters.

### `screens.flow`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow"></a>
The flow of the screen: an XState v5 machine config.

### `screens.flow.id`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-id"></a>
The id of the machine: one segment, which the app gives its machine and an id target `#<id>.<state>` names; absent, the screen id. See the rule `flow-states`.

### `screens.flow.description`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-description"></a>
What the flow does as a whole that no single state says: how its areas or its modes go together. Its states, events, guards and effects say the rest. At most 200 characters.

### `screens.flow.initial`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-initial"></a>
The state the screen opens in.

### `screens.flow.meta`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-meta"></a>
What the contract says of the flow beyond XState: its guards.

### `screens.flow.meta.guards`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-meta-guards"></a>
The guards by name, each with what it holds.

### `screens.flow.meta.guards.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-meta-guards-name"></a>
What a guard holds: a sentence, the failure codes it holds for, or a guard the loader derives from the screen's password pair.

### `screens.flow.meta.guards.<name>.codes`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-meta-guards-name-codes"></a>
The codes it holds for: the action failed with one of them.

### `screens.flow.meta.guards.<name>.confirms`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-meta-guards-name-confirms"></a>
What it holds of the confirmation against the input the confirmation confirms: `differs`, `strays` or `follows`, each the meaning of the rule `pair-differs`, `pair-strays` or `pair-follows`.

### `screens.flow.meta.guards.<name>.composite`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-meta-guards-name-composite"></a>
The input the confirmation confirms breaks a rule of its composite: the meaning of the rule `composite-breaks`.

### `screens.flow.states`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states"></a>
The states by name: plain states, and parallel states of regions.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name"></a>
One state of the flow.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.description`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-description"></a>
What a tester or a user tells apart in the state that its events, effects and `meta.renders` do not say. At most 200 characters.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.type`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-type"></a>
The flow ends in this state.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.meta`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-meta"></a>
What the contract says of the state beyond XState: its address and the anchors it renders.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.meta.address`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-meta-address"></a>
The route that reaches this state: a link or a reload restores it.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.meta.address.path`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-meta-address-path"></a>
The path of a binding of the screen.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.meta.address.search`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-meta-address-search"></a>
Search parameters of the binding, each a literal (`$id` for a minted id).

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.meta.address.search.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-meta-address-search-name"></a>
A string, a number or a boolean.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.meta.renders`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-meta-renders"></a>
Anchors only some states render. An anchor no state lists, nor any it is under, renders in all; else only where it or one above it is listed. A part renders with its base. See the rule `renders`.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.entry`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-entry"></a>
The effects on entering the state.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.entry.type`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-entry-type"></a>
The effect: an anchor shows a code.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.entry.params`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-entry-params"></a>
Which anchor, and the codes it may show.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.entry.params.anchor`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-entry-params-anchor"></a>
The anchor that shows the code.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.entry.params.codes`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-entry-params-codes"></a>
The codes it may show: its data-code is one of them.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.entry.params.to`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-entry-params-to"></a>
The path of the binding the app moves to.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.entry.params.search`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-entry-params-search"></a>
Search parameters of the binding, each a literal (`$id` for a minted id), `{ anchor }`, the value a field of the screen holds, or `{ item }`, the data-id of the entry whose control the user acted on.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.entry.params.search.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-entry-params-search-name"></a>
A string, a number or a boolean.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.entry.params.search.<name>.anchor`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-entry-params-search-name-anchor"></a>
A field of the screen, declared on it.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.entry.params.search.<name>.item`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-entry-params-search-name-item"></a>
An item or a row of the screen, with a `dataId`.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.exit`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-exit"></a>
The effects on leaving the state.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.exit.type`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-exit-type"></a>
The effect: an anchor shows a code.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.exit.params`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-exit-params"></a>
Which anchor, and the codes it may show.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.exit.params.anchor`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-exit-params-anchor"></a>
The anchor that shows the code.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.exit.params.codes`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-exit-params-codes"></a>
The codes it may show: its data-code is one of them.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.exit.params.to`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-exit-params-to"></a>
The path of the binding the app moves to.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.exit.params.search`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-exit-params-search"></a>
Search parameters of the binding, each a literal (`$id` for a minted id), `{ anchor }`, the value a field of the screen holds, or `{ item }`, the data-id of the entry whose control the user acted on.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.exit.params.search.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-exit-params-search-name"></a>
A string, a number or a boolean.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.exit.params.search.<name>.anchor`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-exit-params-search-name-anchor"></a>
A field of the screen, declared on it.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.exit.params.search.<name>.item`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-exit-params-search-name-item"></a>
An item or a row of the screen, with a `dataId`.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.on`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-on"></a>
Per event, what follows: an anchor (the user acts on it), a platform event of platform.events, or a data event of a field the screen shows.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.on.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-on-name"></a>
One transition, or a list of guarded ones, the unguarded one last.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.target`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-on-name-target"></a>
The state the machine moves to; absent: it stays where it is.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.guard`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-on-name-guard"></a>
A guard: a camelCase name of the flow `meta.guards`.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.read`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-on-name-read"></a>
No read after this outcome: it changes nothing a list of this screen shows. On an invoke's `onDone`/`onError` branch, never beside a `read` or under `read: false`. See the rule `read-after-an-action`.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.actions`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-on-name-actions"></a>
The effects, in order.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.actions.type`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-on-name-actions-type"></a>
The effect: an anchor shows a code.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.actions.params`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-on-name-actions-params"></a>
Which anchor, and the codes it may show.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.actions.params.anchor`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-on-name-actions-params-anchor"></a>
The anchor that shows the code.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.actions.params.codes`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-on-name-actions-params-codes"></a>
The codes it may show: its data-code is one of them.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.actions.params.to`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-on-name-actions-params-to"></a>
The path of the binding the app moves to.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.actions.params.search`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-on-name-actions-params-search"></a>
Search parameters of the binding, each a literal (`$id` for a minted id), `{ anchor }`, the value a field of the screen holds, or `{ item }`, the data-id of the entry whose control the user acted on.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.actions.params.search.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-on-name-actions-params-search-name"></a>
A string, a number or a boolean.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.on.<name>.actions.params.search.<name>.anchor`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-on-name-actions-params-search-name-anchor"></a>
A field of the screen, declared on it.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.onError.target`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-onerror-target"></a>
The state the machine moves to; absent: it stays where it is.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.onError.guard`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-onerror-guard"></a>
A guard: a camelCase name of the flow `meta.guards`.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.onError.read`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-onerror-read"></a>
No read after this outcome: it changes nothing a list of this screen shows. On an invoke's `onDone`/`onError` branch, never beside a `read` or under `read: false`. See the rule `read-after-an-action`.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.onError.actions`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-onerror-actions"></a>
The effects, in order.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.onError.actions.type`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-onerror-actions-type"></a>
The effect: an anchor shows a code.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.onError.actions.params`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-onerror-actions-params"></a>
Which anchor, and the codes it may show.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.onError.actions.params.anchor`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-onerror-actions-params-anchor"></a>
The anchor that shows the code.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.onError.actions.params.codes`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-onerror-actions-params-codes"></a>
The codes it may show: its data-code is one of them.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.onError.actions.params.to`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-onerror-actions-params-to"></a>
The path of the binding the app moves to.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.onError.actions.params.search`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-onerror-actions-params-search"></a>
Search parameters of the binding, each a literal (`$id` for a minted id), `{ anchor }`, the value a field of the screen holds, or `{ item }`, the data-id of the entry whose control the user acted on.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.onError.actions.params.search.<name>`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-onerror-actions-params-search-name"></a>
A string, a number or a boolean.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.onError.actions.params.search.<name>.anchor`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-onerror-actions-params-search-name-anchor"></a>
A field of the screen, declared on it.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.onError.actions.params.search.<name>.item`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-onerror-actions-params-search-name-item"></a>
An item or a row of the screen, with a `dataId`.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.onError.description`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-onerror-description"></a>
What follows that the target, the guard and the effects do not say: where a success leads, what a failure shows, what restarts what. At most 200 characters.

### `screens.flow.states.<name>.states.<name>.states.<name>.invoke.read`

<a id="manifest-screens-flow-states-name-states-name-states-name-invoke-read"></a>
Nothing reads after this action: it changes nothing a list of the screen shows. An invoke that says it and reads in one of its outcomes is refused.

### `screens.wireframe`

<a id="manifest-screens-wireframe"></a>
A text sketch of the layout: the default state of the screen in Playwright's aria snapshot grammar.

### `routeParams`

<a id="manifest-routeparams"></a>
The path parameters by route path, for the routes that have any.

### `routeParams.<name>`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name"></a>
A JSON Schema 2020-12 object of flat primitive parameters.

### `routeParams.<name>.type`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-type"></a>
Always "object".

### `routeParams.<name>.properties`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties"></a>
The parameters by name.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name"></a>
One flat parameter.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.type`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-type"></a>
The primitive type of the parameter.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.enum`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-enum"></a>
The values the parameter takes.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.default`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-default"></a>
The value when the URL leaves it out.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.examples`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-examples"></a>
Fake values of the parameter, each one it accepts; the first fills the example link.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.minimum`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-minimum"></a>
The smallest value of a number or an integer.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.maximum`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-maximum"></a>
The largest value of a number or an integer.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.minLength`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-minlength"></a>
The fewest characters of a string.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.maxLength`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-maxlength"></a>
The most characters of a string.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.pattern`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-pattern"></a>
A regular expression (ECMA-262) every value of a string matches.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.description`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-description"></a>
What the route parameter means. Its type, bounds, default and examples say the rest. At most 200 characters.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.x-perch`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-x-perch"></a>
The controls of the page that set the parameter.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.x-perch.control`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-x-perch-control"></a>
The anchor of the control that sets the parameter.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.x-perch.values`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-x-perch-values"></a>
Per enum value, the control that chooses it.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-x-perch-values-name"></a>
How a page sets one enum value of the parameter.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>.control`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-x-perch-values-name-control"></a>
The anchor of the control that chooses this value.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>.controlValue`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-x-perch-values-name-controlvalue"></a>
The data-value of that control which chooses this value.

### `routeParams.<name>.properties.<name>.x-perch.values.<name>.note`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-properties-name-x-perch-values-name-note"></a>
Anything a reader needs to know about the value.

### `routeParams.<name>.required`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-required"></a>
The parameters the route needs.

### `routeParams.<name>.additionalProperties`

<a id="manifest-routeparams-name-additionalproperties"></a>
Always false: no other parameter.

### `ids`

<a id="manifest-ids"></a>
Every declared anchor by name; a derived one is its base plus a suffix.

### `ids.<name>`

<a id="manifest-ids-name"></a>
One declared anchor, as a test looks it up.

### `ids.<name>.kind`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-kind"></a>
The kind of the anchor.

### `ids.<name>.screen`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-screen"></a>
The id of the screen that declares the anchor.

### `ids.<name>.binding`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-binding"></a>
What the anchor does or shows: a field of the model, an action, a route, or (on a field that holds no secret) the search parameter it starts with. See the rules `anchor-binding` and `flow-effects`.

### `ids.<name>.binding.model`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-binding-model"></a>
`entity.field`: a field of an entity of model.schema.json.

### `ids.<name>.binding.action`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-binding-action"></a>
`entity.action` (an action of the entity x-perch) or `platform.<name>` (an action of platform.actions).

### `ids.<name>.binding.params`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-binding-params"></a>
Per parameter of the action, the anchor whose value it takes, a constant, or a search parameter of the route.

### `ids.<name>.binding.params.<name>`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-binding-params-name"></a>
An anchor (the control whose value it takes), a constant `{ "value": ... }`, or a search parameter of the screen's route `{ "search": "<name>" }`.

### `ids.<name>.binding.params.<name>.value`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-binding-params-name-value"></a>
The constant: a string, a number or a boolean.

### `ids.<name>.binding.params.<name>.search`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-binding-params-name-search"></a>
The name of a search parameter every route of the screen declares.

### `ids.<name>.binding.route`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-binding-route"></a>
The path of a binding the anchor navigates to.

### `ids.<name>.binding.search`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-binding-search"></a>
The name of a search parameter every route of the screen declares.

### `ids.<name>.codes`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-codes"></a>
The data-code values the element carries.

### `ids.<name>.ranked`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-ranked"></a>
The message shows one of its codes: of those raised before it shows, the first in the order of `codes`; the others go with it, and a clear of it, in any flow, takes away every code raised before it.

### `ids.<name>.fields`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-fields"></a>
An error's codes, each with the field it concerns, or `form`, the form as a whole.

### `ids.<name>.fields.<name>`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-fields-name"></a>
A field of the screen, by its anchor, or `form`, the form as a whole.

### `ids.<name>.dataId`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-dataid"></a>
What the data-id of the element holds.

### `ids.<name>.dataValue`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-datavalue"></a>
The element, or its derived options, carry a data-value of the choice values.

### `ids.<name>.states`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-states"></a>
The data-state values the element takes.

### `ids.<name>.derived`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-derived"></a>
The derived suffixes the element renders.

### `ids.<name>.roles`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-roles"></a>
The roles that see the element, among those its screen's bindings serve.

### `ids.<name>.own`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-own"></a>
Shown to a member only on the entries it owns.

### `ids.<name>.with`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-with"></a>
It renders only while each of these renders (an anchor, or one showing these codes).

### `ids.<name>.with.<name>`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-with-name"></a>
A code of the vocabulary (data-code).

### `ids.<name>.without`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-without"></a>
It renders only while none of these renders (an anchor, or one showing these codes).

### `ids.<name>.without.<name>`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-without-name"></a>
A code of the vocabulary (data-code).

### `ids.<name>.asks`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-asks"></a>
Derived: the control asks the frame's confirmation before its destructive action runs. A screen file never writes it. See the rule `destructive-question`.

### `ids.<name>.asks.dialog`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-asks-dialog"></a>
The frame's confirmation that asks.

### `ids.<name>.asks.code`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-asks-code"></a>
The code it asks with: what the action destroys.

### `ids.<name>.leaves`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-leaves"></a>
Derived: the control's event moves its flow to another screen, so it asks the frame's leave question first, as a link does.

### `ids.<name>.leaves.dialog`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-leaves-dialog"></a>
The frame's dialog that asks whether to leave and lose what was typed.

### `ids.<name>.leaves.loses`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-leaves-loses"></a>
The fields of its screen that hold input and that the move does not carry in its search: it asks while one of them holds unsaved input.

### `ids.<name>.composite`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-composite"></a>
The platform composite the secret input opts into (`platform.composites.<name>`): its list and items are anchors of the same screen, each marked `derivedFrom`.

### `ids.<name>.derivedFrom`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-derivedfrom"></a>
Derived: the anchor is the list, or one item, of a platform composite a field of the screen opted into; a screen file never writes it.

### `ids.<name>.derivedFrom.composite`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-derivedfrom-composite"></a>
The composite that derives the anchor.

### `ids.<name>.derivedFrom.item`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-derivedfrom-item"></a>
The item of the composite the anchor stands for; absent: the list.

### `ids.<name>.fieldStates`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-fieldstates"></a>
The data-state values the field takes about its input, `unsaved` and `saved`.

### `ids.<name>.holdsInput`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-holdsinput"></a>
The field holds input: the app is to ask before a leave that would lose what is typed into it.

### `ids.<name>.marksState`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-marksstate"></a>
The field shows no state colour: the app leaves its border uncoloured; its `fieldStates` still hold.

### `ids.<name>.secret`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-secret"></a>
The field holds a secret (a password): it never leaves the page but in the request of the action that takes it, never in an address.

### `ids.<name>.value`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-value"></a>
On an `input` or a `textarea`, the value the field takes, derived: the loosest of the values of its model binding and of the action parameters that take it. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `ids.<name>.value.type`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-value-type"></a>
The type of the value: `string`, `integer`, `number`, `boolean`.

### `ids.<name>.value.enum`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-value-enum"></a>
The values it takes, each a string or a number.

### `ids.<name>.value.minLength`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-value-minlength"></a>
The fewest characters of a string.

### `ids.<name>.value.maxLength`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-value-maxlength"></a>
The most characters of a string.

### `ids.<name>.value.pattern`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-value-pattern"></a>
A regular expression (ECMA-262) every value of a string matches.

### `ids.<name>.value.format`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-value-format"></a>
A string value's format: `email`. Its editable field is `type=email` in a form without `noValidate`; an action run without submitting skips the browser's check. See the rule `email-format`.

### `ids.<name>.value.minimum`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-value-minimum"></a>
The smallest value of a number or an integer.

### `ids.<name>.value.maximum`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-value-maximum"></a>
The largest value of a number or an integer.

### `ids.<name>.value.readOnly`

<a id="manifest-ids-name-value-readonly"></a>
The value is a field of the model that is read-only (`x-perch.readonly`).

### `platform`

<a id="manifest-platform"></a>
The platform section of `contract/domain/platform.yaml`, each value with its length resolved: the default taken where it names none (`contract.defaults`), a `null` left out.

### `platform.defaults`

<a id="manifest-platform-defaults"></a>
What a value takes where it writes nothing of its own, said once for every value; from version 6. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `platform.defaults.maxLength`

<a id="manifest-platform-defaults-maxlength"></a>
Default for writable non-enum strings: platform value, model field or constrained inline param (no `value`). Own limit wins; platform `maxLength: null` opts out. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `platform.values`

<a id="manifest-platform-values"></a>
The values by name, each declared once where its policy lives (`email`): a parameter of an action takes one by `value: <name>`. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `platform.values.<name>`

<a id="manifest-platform-values-name"></a>
A value declared once: its type and its constraints, which every parameter that refers to it by name takes. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `platform.values.<name>.type`

<a id="manifest-platform-values-name-type"></a>
The primitive type of the value.

### `platform.values.<name>.enum`

<a id="manifest-platform-values-name-enum"></a>
The values it takes, each a string or a number.

### `platform.values.<name>.minLength`

<a id="manifest-platform-values-name-minlength"></a>
The fewest characters of a string.

### `platform.values.<name>.maxLength`

<a id="manifest-platform-values-name-maxlength"></a>
The most characters of a string; from version 6 a string value that writes none, no `enum`, takes `defaults.maxLength`, and `null` takes no limit. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `platform.values.<name>.pattern`

<a id="manifest-platform-values-name-pattern"></a>
A regular expression (ECMA-262) every value of a string matches.

### `platform.values.<name>.format`

<a id="manifest-platform-values-name-format"></a>
A string value's format: `email`. Its editable field is `type=email` in a form without `noValidate`; an action run without submitting skips the browser's check. See the rule `email-format`.

### `platform.values.<name>.minimum`

<a id="manifest-platform-values-name-minimum"></a>
The smallest value of a number or an integer.

### `platform.values.<name>.maximum`

<a id="manifest-platform-values-name-maximum"></a>
The largest value of a number or an integer.

### `platform.values.<name>.description`

<a id="manifest-platform-values-name-description"></a>
What the value holds, for every parameter that refers to it. At most 200 characters.

### `platform.values.<name>.examples`

<a id="manifest-platform-values-name-examples"></a>
Fake values the value accepts, for a test or an example to use: any list.

### `platform.actions`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions"></a>
The platform actions by name: `platform.<name>` in a binding.

### `platform.actions.<name>`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name"></a>
One platform action.

### `platform.actions.<name>.description`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-description"></a>
What the platform action does. What holds once it succeeds is its `result`, and its parameters, codes, roles and mail say the rest. At most 200 characters.

### `platform.actions.<name>.params`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-params"></a>
The parameters by name.

### `platform.actions.<name>.params.<name>`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-params-name"></a>
One parameter of a platform action: it writes its `type` or the `value` it refers to, and may write the keywords of a value beside it, each only narrowing the value. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `platform.actions.<name>.params.<name>.type`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-params-name-type"></a>
The type of the value: string, boolean, number; left out where `value` gives it.

### `platform.actions.<name>.params.<name>.value`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-params-name-value"></a>
The value the parameter takes: a name of `platform.values` or a model field, `<entity>.<field>`, with its constraints; a keyword beside it may narrow them, never widen. See the rule `declared-values`.

### `platform.actions.<name>.params.<name>.enum`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-params-name-enum"></a>
The values it takes, each a string or a number.

### `platform.actions.<name>.params.<name>.minLength`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-params-name-minlength"></a>
The fewest characters of a string.

### `platform.actions.<name>.params.<name>.maxLength`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-params-name-maxlength"></a>
The most characters of a string.

### `platform.actions.<name>.params.<name>.pattern`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-params-name-pattern"></a>
A regular expression (ECMA-262) every value of a string matches.

### `platform.actions.<name>.params.<name>.format`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-params-name-format"></a>
A string value's format: `email`. Its editable field is `type=email` in a form without `noValidate`; an action run without submitting skips the browser's check. See the rule `email-format`.

### `platform.actions.<name>.params.<name>.minimum`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-params-name-minimum"></a>
The smallest value of a number or an integer.

### `platform.actions.<name>.params.<name>.maximum`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-params-name-maximum"></a>
The largest value of a number or an integer.

### `platform.actions.<name>.params.<name>.optional`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-params-name-optional"></a>
The action runs without the parameter: a field that passes it may stay empty.

### `platform.actions.<name>.params.<name>.codes`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-params-name-codes"></a>
The codes of the action whose failure concerns this parameter, each one of its `codes`, beyond those named after it (`<parameter>-<suffix>`). See the rule `attributed-codes`.

### `platform.actions.<name>.params.<name>.description`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-params-name-description"></a>
What the parameter is for. Its type or the value it refers to, and its constraints, are written beside it. At most 200 characters.

### `platform.actions.<name>.result`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-result"></a>
What holds once the action runs: one outcome, or a list of outcome lines, the plain success first. A refusal is its code's condition. See the rule `action-outcomes`.

### `platform.actions.<name>.codes`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-codes"></a>
The codes a failure of the action shows; from version 3 each that ends like the code of a field concerns a parameter. See the rules `action-outcomes` and `attributed-codes`.

### `platform.actions.<name>.codes.<name>`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-codes-name"></a>
When the action answers with the code, as the server judges it. A rule several actions keep is an invariant, named under the code with or instead of it. At most 200 characters.

### `platform.actions.<name>.codes.<name>.invariant`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-codes-name-invariant"></a>
The invariant, by its name under `platform.invariants`, or a list of two or more when several refuse with this code; each has this code as its `refuses`.

### `platform.actions.<name>.codes.<name>.condition`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-codes-name-condition"></a>
A cause of the code that is no invariant, beside the ones named: when else the action answers with it. At most 200 characters.

### `platform.actions.<name>.roles`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-roles"></a>
The roles that may run the action.

### `platform.actions.<name>.paged`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-paged"></a>
The action answers one page: number parameters `page`, from 1, and `size`, one of its sizes; the page in its result's order, with the total the other parameters match. See the rule `paged-list`.

### `platform.actions.<name>.paged.sizes`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-paged-sizes"></a>
The sizes a page may have, each once, the smallest first.

### `platform.actions.<name>.paged.default`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-paged-default"></a>
The size of a page the route does not name, a size.

### `platform.actions.<name>.changes`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-changes"></a>
Required: the list, section or table `source` actions its success changes in its context; never itself, nor a switch of organisation or account. `[]`: none. See the rule `read-after-an-action`.

### `platform.actions.<name>.mail`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-mail"></a>
The mail the action sends, when it sends one.

### `platform.actions.<name>.mail.to`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-mail-to"></a>
The parameter of the action whose value the mail goes to, or `account`: the email of the account the action acts on. See the rule `mail-recipients`.

### `platform.actions.<name>.mail.language`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-mail-language"></a>
Where the mail's language comes from: a language parameter, `account`, `organisation` or `recipient` (never last), or a list of them, the first that has one speaking. See the rule `mail-recipients`.

### `platform.actions.<name>.mail.link`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-mail-link"></a>
Where the mail's link leads.

### `platform.actions.<name>.mail.link.screen`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-mail-link-screen"></a>
The screen the link opens, at the path of its route.

### `platform.actions.<name>.mail.link.search`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-mail-link-search"></a>
The token's route search parameter. From version 4 the loader adds it to each route from the same-named platform value (`type`, no `format`); screen files omit it. See the rule `mail-link-search`.

### `platform.actions.<name>.notices`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-notices"></a>
The notices the action sends, when it sends any: mails that only tell, with no token, each by its name.

### `platform.actions.<name>.notices.name`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-notices-name"></a>
The name of the notice, unique within the action: `password-changed`; a scenario names it `[notice:<action>.<name>]`.

### `platform.actions.<name>.notices.to`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-notices-to"></a>
The parameter of the action whose value the notice goes to, or `account`: the email of the account the action acts on, as it was before the action. See the rule `mail-recipients`.

### `platform.actions.<name>.notices.language`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-notices-language"></a>
Where the mail's language comes from: a language parameter of the action, or `account` (the account the action acts on). See the rule `mail-recipients`.

### `platform.actions.<name>.notices.link`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-notices-link"></a>
The plain link of the notice, when it has one: a password changed links to the reset of a password, if it was not the account's owner.

### `platform.actions.<name>.notices.link.screen`

<a id="manifest-platform-actions-name-notices-link-screen"></a>
The screen the link opens, at a route that needs no parameter: the link carries no token and no search.

### `platform.invariants`

<a id="manifest-platform-invariants"></a>
The invariants by name, each once: its rule with its values written in, the code it refuses with, its values and the actions it limits, derived. See the rule `action-outcomes`.

### `platform.invariants.<name>.rule`

<a id="manifest-platform-invariants-name-rule"></a>
Derived: the rule as written, each `{name}` of its values written in. See the rule `action-outcomes`.

### `platform.invariants.<name>.refuses`

<a id="manifest-platform-invariants-name-refuses"></a>
The code an action refuses with when the rule would break: each action names the invariant under it.

### `platform.invariants.<name>.values`

<a id="manifest-platform-invariants-name-values"></a>
The numbers the rule states, by camelCase name: a count or a duration; the rule writes each as `{name}`. See the rule `action-outcomes`.

### `platform.invariants.<name>.values.<name>`

<a id="manifest-platform-invariants-name-values-name"></a>
A count: a limit, how many.

### `platform.invariants.<name>.values.<name>.seconds`

<a id="manifest-platform-invariants-name-values-name-seconds"></a>
The duration in seconds.

### `platform.invariants.<name>.values.<name>.minutes`

<a id="manifest-platform-invariants-name-values-name-minutes"></a>
The duration in minutes.

### `platform.invariants.<name>.values.<name>.hours`

<a id="manifest-platform-invariants-name-values-name-hours"></a>
The duration in hours.

### `platform.invariants.<name>.actions`

<a id="manifest-platform-invariants-name-actions"></a>
Derived: the actions that name the invariant under the code it refuses with, in the order of the platform file. See the rule `action-outcomes`.

### `platform.events`

<a id="manifest-platform-events"></a>
The events the platform raises, by name, each with what it means: `platform.<name>` as an event of a flow.

### `platform.events.<name>`

<a id="manifest-platform-events-name"></a>
What the event means: when the platform raises it. What follows is said by the flows that handle it. At most 200 characters.

### `platform.leaving`

<a id="manifest-platform-leaving"></a>
Actions (named as in a binding) that drop typed input, e.g. a sign-out. Ask first only when `unsavedInput.actions` is `question` and a field holds unsaved input. See the rule `leave-questions`.

### `platform.toasts`

<a id="manifest-platform-toasts"></a>
The platform actions whose success toasts, each with its toast's code (`organisations.create: organisation-created`); an unlisted platform action never toasts. See the rule `success-toast`.

### `platform.toasts.<name>`

<a id="manifest-platform-toasts-name"></a>
A code of the vocabulary (data-code).

### `platform.composites`

<a id="manifest-platform-composites"></a>
The composites by name: a list with its items the platform declares once (`password-rules`), derived into every screen whose secret input writes `composite: <name>`.

### `platform.composites.<name>`

<a id="manifest-platform-composites-name"></a>
A composite the platform declares once where its policy lives: a list with its items, derived into every screen whose field opts in with `composite: <name>`.

### `platform.composites.<name>.description`

<a id="manifest-platform-composites-name-description"></a>
What the composite is for: the list it derives on a screen carries it, and each item has a description of its own. At most 200 characters.

### `platform.composites.<name>.list`

<a id="manifest-platform-composites-name-list"></a>
The last segment of the list the composite derives on a screen, `<screen>.<list>`: a `list`, so it ends with `-list` (`password-rule-list`).

### `platform.composites.<name>.itemState`

<a id="manifest-platform-composites-name-itemstate"></a>
The data-state each item takes once what it says holds: a row state of the vocabulary (`met`).

### `platform.composites.<name>.items`

<a id="manifest-platform-composites-name-items"></a>
The items by name, each with its description: the screen gets `<screen>.<list>.<name>-item`, an `item` with the item state.

### `platform.composites.<name>.items.<name>`

<a id="manifest-platform-composites-name-items-name"></a>
What the item of the composite's list says: every screen that opts in derives an item anchor with it as its description, and the composite's `itemState` marks it. At most 200 characters.

### `platform.unsavedInput`

<a id="manifest-platform-unsavedinput"></a>
Every leave that would lose what the user typed and did not save, and how the app asks first: through the question, by the browser, never, or it cannot.

### `platform.unsavedInput.question`

<a id="manifest-platform-unsavedinput-question"></a>
The reserved dialog that asks in the app whether to leave and lose what was typed: its code names what it asks, its confirm-button leaves, its cancel-button stays.

### `platform.unsavedInput.screen`

<a id="manifest-platform-unsavedinput-screen"></a>
A change of screen the user starts: a link, a control whose flow moves to another screen (the loader marks it `leaves`), Back, Forward.

### `platform.unsavedInput.actions`

<a id="manifest-platform-unsavedinput-actions"></a>
A control bound to an action of `leaving`, before the action runs.

### `platform.unsavedInput.unload`

<a id="manifest-platform-unsavedinput-unload"></a>
A reload, closing the tab or the window, an address typed by hand: the browser's own prompt, drawn outside the page, so no anchor or code marks it.

### `platform.unsavedInput.state`

<a id="manifest-platform-unsavedinput-state"></a>
A change of state inside one screen: a tab, a search parameter, a form opened or closed.

### `platform.unsavedInput.flow`

<a id="manifest-platform-unsavedinput-flow"></a>
A navigation a flow makes after its action completed: the action took the input.

### `platform.unsavedInput.elsewhere`

<a id="manifest-platform-unsavedinput-elsewhere"></a>
A leave nobody started here: another tab or device signed the account in or out, a database that will not open, a flow moving on a platform or data event. Nothing here can keep what was typed.

### `platform.reserved`

<a id="manifest-platform-reserved"></a>
The anchors the shell owns; a feature never declares them.

### `platform.status`

<a id="manifest-platform-status"></a>
The readiness line of the app.

### `platform.status.anchor`

<a id="manifest-platform-status-anchor"></a>
The anchor of the readiness line of the app.

### `platform.status.states`

<a id="manifest-platform-status-states"></a>
The app states it takes, in order.

### `activities`

<a id="manifest-activities"></a>
The activities of `contract/domain/activities/`, verbatim: machines whose states are visits to screens.

### `activities.id`

<a id="manifest-activities-id"></a>
The id of the activity, the name of its file: `session`.

### `activities.title`

<a id="manifest-activities-title"></a>
The title of the activity, for a reader.

### `activities.description`

<a id="manifest-activities-description"></a>
What the activity is for: the task it serves across its screens. Its states are the visits, and the screens do the work. At most 200 characters.

### `activities.roles`

<a id="manifest-activities-roles"></a>
The roles that take part in the activity.

### `activities.data`

<a id="manifest-activities-data"></a>
What the activity carries from screen to screen, by name.

### `activities.data.<name>`

<a id="manifest-activities-data-name"></a>
One thing the activity carries, and the field of the model it is.

### `activities.data.<name>.description`

<a id="manifest-activities-data-name-description"></a>
What the activity carries from screen to screen; `model` names the field of the model it is. At most 200 characters.

### `activities.data.<name>.model`

<a id="manifest-activities-data-name-model"></a>
`entity.field`: a field of an entity of model.schema.json.

### `activities.initial`

<a id="manifest-activities-initial"></a>
The state the activity starts in.

### `activities.meta`

<a id="manifest-activities-meta"></a>
What the contract says of the machine beyond XState: its guards.

### `activities.meta.guards`

<a id="manifest-activities-meta-guards"></a>
The guards by name, each with what it holds.

### `activities.meta.guards.<name>`

<a id="manifest-activities-meta-guards-name"></a>
What the guard holds, in one sentence the app implements. At most 200 characters.

### `activities.states`

<a id="manifest-activities-states"></a>
The states by name: the visits.

### `activities.states.<name>`

<a id="manifest-activities-states-name"></a>
One state of the activity: a visit to a screen.

### `activities.states.<name>.description`

<a id="manifest-activities-states-name-description"></a>
What the visit is for in the task. Its `meta` names the screen and the address, and the screen has a description of its own. At most 200 characters.

### `activities.states.<name>.type`

<a id="manifest-activities-states-name-type"></a>
The activity ends in this state.

### `activities.states.<name>.meta`

<a id="manifest-activities-states-name-meta"></a>
What the contract says of the state beyond XState: its screen and address.

### `activities.states.<name>.meta.screen`

<a id="manifest-activities-states-name-meta-screen"></a>
The screen the state is a visit to: a screen id.

### `activities.states.<name>.meta.address`

<a id="manifest-activities-states-name-meta-address"></a>
The route of the visit: the activity is where its address is.

### `activities.states.<name>.meta.address.path`

<a id="manifest-activities-states-name-meta-address-path"></a>
The path of a binding of that screen.

### `activities.states.<name>.on`

<a id="manifest-activities-states-name-on"></a>
Per anchor of the state's screen or of the frame, where acting on it leads.

### `activities.states.<name>.on.<name>`

<a id="manifest-activities-states-name-on-name"></a>
One transition, or a list of guarded ones, the unguarded one last.

### `activities.states.<name>.on.<name>.target`

<a id="manifest-activities-states-name-on-name-target"></a>
The state the activity moves to: the next visit.

### `activities.states.<name>.on.<name>.guard`

<a id="manifest-activities-states-name-on-name-guard"></a>
A guard: a camelCase name of the flow `meta.guards`.

### `activities.states.<name>.on.<name>.description`

<a id="manifest-activities-states-name-on-name-description"></a>
What acting on the anchor leads to that the target and the guard do not say; the flows of the screens do the work. At most 200 characters.

### `scenarios`

<a id="manifest-scenarios"></a>
The end-to-end scenarios, parsed from their files under `tests/e2e/scenarios/`: the id is the path of a file without `.md`.

### `scenarios.id`

<a id="manifest-scenarios-id"></a>
`<entity>/<layer>/<slug>` or `<entity>/<layer>/<area>/<slug>`, the layer one of `base`, `flows`, `features`: the path of `tests/e2e/scenarios/<id>.md` and of its test. See the rule `scenario-files`.

### `scenarios.title`

<a id="manifest-scenarios-title"></a>
What the scenario checks, in a few words.

### `scenarios.tags`

<a id="manifest-scenarios-tags"></a>
The test tags: `smoke` or `full`.

### `scenarios.activity`

<a id="manifest-scenarios-activity"></a>
The activity of `contract/domain/activities/` the scenario walks, by its id.

### `scenarios.devices`

<a id="manifest-scenarios-devices"></a>
A letter per browser, each with the role it acts as.

### `scenarios.devices.<name>`

<a id="manifest-scenarios-devices-name"></a>
A role of the contract: of its terms file, or of the vocabulary's Roles.

### `scenarios.where`

<a id="manifest-scenarios-where"></a>
Where the scenario starts, with bracket references.

### `scenarios.data`

<a id="manifest-scenarios-data"></a>
The test data by name: `generate` or a fixed value.

### `scenarios.scenario`

<a id="manifest-scenarios-scenario"></a>
The scenario in one sentence.

### `scenarios.steps`

<a id="manifest-scenarios-steps"></a>
The steps, with bracket references.

### `scenarios.expected`

<a id="manifest-scenarios-expected"></a>
The expected results, with bracket references.

### `model`

<a id="manifest-model"></a>
`contract/domain/model.schema.json`: the data model as JSON Schema 2020-12, verbatim but each field that names no limit with the default length taken (`contract.defaults`).

### `model.<name>`

<a id="manifest-model-name"></a>
A keyword of the model document.

### `rules`

<a id="manifest-rules"></a>
The texts of the rules this manifest and its schema name by id, each id in backticks after "rule" or "rules"; the reference is not shipped. A rule only these texts name is not carried.

### `rules.<name>`

<a id="manifest-rules-name"></a>
One rule of the engine.

### `rules.<name>.title`

<a id="manifest-rules-name-title"></a>
The title of the rule.

### `rules.<name>.text`

<a id="manifest-rules-name-text"></a>
The rule as the reference writes it, ending with a line "Keys it concerns": its links point into the reference, which the manifest does not carry, so read each key by its name.

## Rules

### Anchors: the grammar and one literal per name

<a id="rule-anchor-grammar"></a>
An anchor is the value of `data-testid`: 2 to 4 dot-separated kebab-case segments, the screen name first (`user.sign-in`, `organisation`) and the kind last, as the last dash-separated word of the last segment. Each segment is a kebab-case word group (`[a-z][a-z0-9]*(-[a-z0-9]+)*`); the first one or two segments are a screen name from the vocabulary's "Screen names in use" (the longest match on segment boundaries wins), and the last is `<object>-<kind>` or just `<kind>`. `user.sign-in.email-input` is an `input` of the sign-in screen, `organisation.notes.item` an `item` of the organisation screen. The kinds are a closed list in the vocabulary, each with the Playwright role it maps to. An anchor names what the element is for: never its text, its colour, its position or an entity's id.

A derived anchor adds one suffix from the vocabulary's closed table for a part a composite renders itself: `user.profile.sign-out-dialog.confirm-button`, `app-shell.locale-select.option`. Code builds it only with `testIdFor(base, suffix)` from `@workspace/ui-contract/anchors`, and the loaded base lists the suffix in its `derived`: a file writes it there, except for the parts a kind brings by itself from version 4 (see the rule `kind-parts`) and a paged list's parts (see the rule `paged-list`), which the loader lists alone. A rendered anchor, base or derived, has up to 5 segments and matches `^[a-z][a-z0-9]*(-[a-z0-9]+)*(\.[a-z][a-z0-9]*(-[a-z0-9]+)*){1,4}$`. Every derived suffix ends with a kind of the vocabulary, the kind of the part it marks. A known anchor is a declared anchor, a declared base followed by a suffix of the vocabulary's table, or a paged list's size options (`isKnownAnchor(anchor, declared, suffixes, paged)` checks the suffix against the table and the base against the declared anchors, and cannot see the base's `derived`; `baseOf(anchor, suffixes)`, `sizeOptionOf(list)`, `isSizeOption(anchor, paged)`); `ANCHOR_PATTERN` and `isAnchor(text)` hold the grammar, `segmentsOf(anchor)` splits an anchor, `kindOf(anchor)` is the last dash-separated word of its last segment, and `screenOf(anchor, screenNames)` the longest screen name on segment boundaries.

One literal per rendered name:

- every anchor is declared once, in one screen file, and written once in the sources;
- no anchor equals another one plus a derived suffix: the entries of `device.list` are `device.list.session-item`, never `device.list.item`, which would read as a part the list renders itself;
- an `item`, `row` or `option` is the one anchor that marks many elements: every entry of its container, told apart by `data-id` or `data-value`.

So a search finds every place a name lives. A grep for the full name of a derived anchor finds every explicit reference to it: the scenarios and tests that use it, a `flow` event or a choice value that names it, a view's unit test that writes it as a literal. Its declaration is found by the base, and so is the view that renders the part, which passes the base to the composite. A part its kind brings by itself has no line of its own in a file from version 4 (before, a file may list it in the base's `derived`): the declaration of the base, whose kind brings it, is where to read it:

```sh
git grep -nF 'user.profile.sign-out-dialog.cancel-button'   # the explicit references
git grep -nF 'user.profile.sign-out-dialog'                 # the declaration and the view too
```

A derived name may have no reference in the screens, the scenarios or the tests at all: `app-shell.locale-select.option` is named only by the vocabulary (its choice values and the reserved row of its base), and `app-shell.locale-select`, a select, declares it: a select brings its `option`s by itself from version 4, and before that a file may list `option` in its `derived`.

In code an anchor is a literal in one of four forms, the only ones the sources check reads:

```tsx
<h1 data-testid="user.profile.heading">{profile.name}</h1>  // the attribute
<ConfirmDialog testId="user.profile.sign-out-dialog" />      // a testId prop
<AppShell navigationTestId="app-shell.nav" />                // a prop named <part>TestId
{ to: '/me', testId: 'app-shell.nav.profile-link' }          // a static testId key
const NOTES_ITEM_TEST_ID = 'organisation.notes.item'         // a <NAME>_TEST_ID constant
```

The first four are in the views today; the constant is the form for one element that two render paths draw, and no view needs it yet. A name, a member such as `props.testId` or a `testIdFor()` call passes on a literal written elsewhere. An anchor built at runtime (a template with `${}`, `+`, a conditional between strings) is a problem: choose between two literals, pass a prop, or derive the part.

Keys it concerns: [`screen.anchors`](#screen-anchors), [`screen.anchors.<name>`](#screen-anchors-name), [`screen.anchors.<name>.kind`](#screen-anchors-name-kind), [`screen.anchors.<name>.derived`](#screen-anchors-name-derived).

### Derived parts: what an anchor's `derived` lists

<a id="rule-derived-parts"></a>
`derived` on an anchor: The derived suffixes the element renders, each from the vocabulary's closed table of derived suffixes and ending with a kind (the kind of the part it marks). The part is `<anchor>.<suffix>`, which code builds only with `testIdFor(base, suffix)` from `@workspace/ui-contract/anchors`; the loaded base lists every suffix it renders here, and a derived part may have no reference in the screens, scenarios or tests at all, since its declaration is found by the base. No declared anchor equals another plus a derived suffix (`<list>.session-item`, never `<list>.item`). The loader lists a paged list's four parts, which a screen file never writes (see the rule `paged-list`). From version 4 so are the parts a kind brings by itself (see the rule `kind-parts`; the eye of a password input is presentation only, see the rule `visibility-button`), and a screen file writes here only a suffix no kind brings. Before version 4 a file may list them here, and a secret `input` alone lists `visibility-button`.

Keys it concerns: [`screen.anchors.<name>.derived`](#screen-anchors-name-derived), [`manifest.screens.anchors.<name>.derived`](#manifest-screens-anchors-name-derived).

### The visibility button of a password input

<a id="rule-visibility-button"></a>
One derived part is presentation only: `visibility-button`, the eye a password input renders beside itself, which shows what the input holds as typed or hides it again. From version 4 the loader derives it on a secret `input` (`secret: true`; see the rule `kind-parts`), and before that the input may list it, `derived: [ visibility-button ]`; only a secret `input` has one: on any other field there is nothing hidden for it to show.

Its `data-value` names what a click does: `show` while the input hides its value, `hide` while it shows it; the two values are the suffix's own (`VISIBILITY_VALUES`), never rows of the vocabulary's Choice values. Whether the field shows its value is held by the field itself, the one transient state a flow never holds, so the button is never an event of a flow or an activity, it is not one of the controls the machine enables, it is always enabled, and it holds no input, so no binding's `params` and no invoke's `input` takes it (`contract:check` refuses each of these, and a `clear` or a `read` naming it). A test locates it as `<input>.visibility-button` and asserts `show` or `hide` on it; the manifest lists it in `contract.derivedParts` with its kind and its values. A scenario may name the values `show` and `hide` once an input renders it.

Keys it concerns: [`screen.anchors.<name>.secret`](#screen-anchors-name-secret), [`screen.anchors.<name>.derived`](#screen-anchors-name-derived).

### A field that holds a secret

<a id="rule-secret-field"></a>
`secret: true` on a field: A field that holds a secret (a password, its confirmation, a current password, anything that never leaves the page but in the request of the action that takes it): no navigate carries it into a search (a `{ anchor }` value would keep it in the address, the browser's history and the logs) and no search parameter starts it (a `{ search }` binding on it is refused); a secret is never filled from a URL. Only a field has it, and only a secret `input` renders a `visibility-button` and opts into a composite. A `clear` of its flow empties it as it empties any field (see the rule `flow-effects`). Its description may be left out: the engine says it holds a secret the user types and never shows, and the manifest appends `(type password)` once to a written one.

Keys it concerns: [`screen.anchors.<name>.secret`](#screen-anchors-name-secret), [`manifest.screens.anchors.<name>.secret`](#manifest-screens-anchors-name-secret).

### Attributes: data-id, data-value, data-state, data-code

<a id="rule-attributes"></a>
- `data-id` on an `item` or a `row` that declares `dataId`, the one static anchor that marks every entry of its list, table or section: the id of the entity each entry shows. An `item` or a `row` named as a binding parameter, as an invoke's `input` or as a navigate's `{ item }` search takes the `data-id` of the entry whose control acts, so it declares `dataId`; the selection may then be in the address, and a reload or a copied link reaches the same entry. A `data-id` is data, not contract: no check reads its value.
- `data-value` on an `option` (a select's derived options included), a `badge` or a `button` that switches a mode: the stable key of a choice that is not an entity, the key the code already uses and never its label. Each fixed value is a row of the vocabulary's Choice values table with its meaning. On a `visibility-button` it is `show` or `hide`, the values of its suffix, never rows of the table; on an option of a paged list's `size-select`, the page size it chooses. A value asserted on an anchor must be one that anchor, or an anchor under it, carries. A select always carries them, so from version 4 the loader derives `dataValue` on it and a file never writes it (see the rule `kind-parts`).
- `data-from`, `data-to` and `data-total` on the `range` of a paged list: the first and the last entry the page shows, from 1 (`0` and `0` when it shows none), and how many the search finds in all.
- `data-state` on a `status`, `list`, `section` or `item`, each a state of the vocabulary: an app state on the frame's `status`, a container state on a `status`, `list` or `section` (`loading` while its data arrives after the page), a row state on an `item`, which an entry that differs from the others or changes in place carries and an entry in its normal state does not. The state of a field is not written here: the manifest lists under `fieldStates` the states of the vocabulary's "Field states" each field may carry, and the rule `field-states-carried` says which field carries which, and when.
- `data-code` on a `message`, `error`, `toast` or `dialog`: the stable code of what it says (see the rule `error-codes-and-fields`).

The page speaks the user's language, so tests and agents never assert its words. They assert the anchor with its code, state, id or value. A behaviour that varies is a code, a state or a value of one anchor, never a second anchor: the sign-in form has one `user.sign-in.toggle-button` whose `data-value` says which way it switches, and one `user.sign-in.error` whose code says what went wrong.

Keys it concerns: [`screen.anchors.<name>.codes`](#screen-anchors-name-codes), [`screen.anchors.<name>.states`](#screen-anchors-name-states), [`screen.anchors.<name>.dataId`](#screen-anchors-name-dataid), [`screen.anchors.<name>.dataValue`](#screen-anchors-name-datavalue), [`manifest.screens.anchors.<name>.dataId`](#manifest-screens-anchors-name-dataid), [`manifest.screens.anchors.<name>.dataValue`](#manifest-screens-anchors-name-datavalue), [`manifest.screens.anchors.<name>.states`](#manifest-screens-anchors-name-states).

### What an anchor is bound to

<a id="rule-anchor-binding"></a>
The `binding` of an anchor says what it does or shows.

`{ model: <entity>.<field> }`: a field of `model.schema.json` (`$defs.<entity>`). `{ action: <entity>.<action>, params: { ... } }`: an action of the entity's `x-perch.actions`; `{ action: platform.<name> }`: an action of the platform section.

Each parameter names the anchor whose value it takes, a constant `{ value: true }`, or `{ search: <name> }`: the value of that search parameter of the route the screen was opened at, which every binding of the screen declares under `search` (the token a link carries). An `item` or a `row` named as a parameter gives the `data-id` of the entry whose control acts: a control (a button, a link, a tab or a field, never a cell or a sibling entry) in the item's part (`<part>.<name>`, like the item), rendered in each entry (`<list>.revoke-button` passes `session: <list>.session-item`, the session of its own row). The item is an entry of a list, a table or a section of its own screen, declared under it or beside it in its part, and declares its `dataId`; an invoke's `input` takes one the same way, in a state that only the events of such controls enter.

`{ route: /path }`: the path of a binding of some screen.

`{ search: <name> }`, on a field (an input, a textarea, a select, a checkbox, a radio or a switch): the field starts with the value of that search parameter of the route the screen was opened at, takes it again each time that parameter becomes another, and the user may change it; every binding of the screen declares it under `search`. It sits in a list beside the action the field gives its value to: the email a link carried into `/forgot-password?email=...` fills the email input the request is sent with, `[ { search: email }, { action: platform.request-password-reset, params: { email: user.forgot-password.email-input } } ]`. A navigate of another screen's flow carries the value there (`{ anchor: <field> }`, see the rule `flow-effects`). A `secret` field never takes it: a secret is never filled from a URL.

A list of bindings means the anchor runs them in that order, or one of them by the mode of the screen; its `flow` says which.

`contract:check` holds every binding to what exists: a field, an action, a route, or a search parameter every route of the screen declares.

Keys it concerns: [`screen.anchors.<name>.binding`](#screen-anchors-name-binding), [`manifest.screens.anchors.<name>.binding`](#manifest-screens-anchors-name-binding), [`manifest.ids.<name>.binding`](#manifest-ids-name-binding).

### The entries of a list and the controls that act on them

<a id="rule-entries-of-a-list"></a>
An `item` or a `row` (`ENTRY_KINDS`) marks every entry of its list, table or section (`LISTING_KINDS`), which `data-id` tells apart. The container an entry belongs to is a list, a table or a section of its screen that it is declared under (`device.list.session-item` under `device.list`), the nearest first, then one declared beside it in its part (`organisation.notes.item` beside `organisation.notes.list`): `containersOf(entry, anchors)`.

A control in the entry's part (`<part>.<name>`, like the entry, and never the entry itself: `inPartOf(entry, control)`) is rendered in each entry and acts on the entry it is in, whose `data-id` it gives. `CONTROL_KINDS` are the kinds a person acts on: a button, a link, a tab and the `FIELD_KINDS` (`input`, `textarea`, `select`, `checkbox`, `radio`, `switch`). `itemIdOf(control, item)` is the `data-id` of the entry of `item` the control is in, the closest element whose anchor is `item`, which a navigate's `{ item }` search takes: the app reads it off the page, so the id that reaches the address is the attribute a test reads, never one the view passes beside it. A control outside such an entry, or an entry without an id, is a defect of the view, never guessed: it throws.

`isUnder(anchor, other)` says `other` is `anchor` or an anchor under it (`<anchor>.<rest>`), and `partOf(anchor)` is the part it sits in, the anchor without its last segment.

Keys it concerns: [`screen.anchors.<name>.dataId`](#screen-anchors-name-dataid), [`screen.anchors.<name>.binding`](#screen-anchors-name-binding).

### Marking a page and locating an element

<a id="rule-marking-a-page"></a>
**Placement.** The anchor goes on the semantic element whose role its kind maps to: the button, the input, the `li` of an entry, the dialog's own box. Never on a layout wrapper, a fragment, a portal or a backdrop: those change when a component is rewritten, the semantic element stays. `ConfirmDialog` puts its anchor on the dialog's popup, not on the portal or the overlay around it. A leaf kit component spreads its props, so the call site writes the literal.

**What needs an anchor.**

- A container that scopes a test: a page `status`, a `form`, a `list`, a `section`, a `dialog`, a `panel`.
- Every entry of a list (`item`, `row`, `option`).
