an index, a call or a construct signature, `any`, `unknown`, a type the file declares more than once or not at its top, or an interface or an alias a file with no `import` or `export` declares (a script's declarations are global and may merge with others; a type literal written in it is still read), cannot be told; and since the file's own declarations are trusted so, no typed file of the repository git lists (`.ts`, `.tsx`, `.mts`, `.cts`; a test, a declaration file and a file outside the source roots, `apps/web/build` among them, included; ignored output not, and not a file of a fixture project that compiles alone, one outside `apps/web/` and `packages/ui/` whose nearest `tsconfig.json` above it lies inside a `__fixtures__` folder, so its stand-in for a kit module reaches no view, while a fixture its package's tsconfig or the repository's compiles, which a source's program may import, and a fixture inside those two are read like any file) may write a `declare module` that names a source file, a relative path that resolves, against the declaring file, into a source root, an absolute path, the web app's `@/` alias or a root's package (`@workspace/web`, `@workspace/ui`), a backslash in it separating its segments as `/` does, the way TypeScript reads it (`'..\\src\\x'` names what `'../src/x'` names): it is refused at its line (`AUGMENTED_MODULE`, `sources/augmentation.ts`), read from the parsed file whatever comment stands between its words, and a file that holds the word `module` but does not parse is refused as unread (`UNREAD_DECLARATIONS`), while an ambient module (`'*.surql'`), a virtual one (`'virtual:perch-build'`), another package's, a relative one that names no file of a root and `declare global` stand), and otherwise possibly its type; another event, one the check cannot read, or alternatives naming different events are a problem, in ahead mode too, where a state the machine can be in renders it (`meta.renders`, `isRendered`) and does not handle it, a parallel state counting as one state of each of its regions, and may carry it where every state handles it. On any other element that carries an anchor so, each of the two is a problem, and so is one written in another idiom, at its line, in ahead mode too, but for one the app still writes in its idiom where the contract has just stopped calling for it: in ahead mode an `aria-invalid` on a field, or a `disabled` on a control of those kinds, passes, so a business commit may move an error's code off a field, or drop a control's event, before the view follows (the check cannot tell a prop the view still writes from one it adds there, so a new one passes the branch too, and the full check refuses it at the landing); one the contract calls for and the element lacks is reported at the anchor's literal, for a part at the first `testIdFor()` of two literals that builds it (except in ahead mode). A part a `testIdFor()` builds from a base passed through is not read.

Coverage markers are read by `ignoreMarkers(text, { program, comments })`: `ui-contract-ignore: <reason>` (the element and its subtree) or `ui-contract-ignore-self: <reason>` (the element alone), as a line comment in code (`// ui-contract-ignore: <reason>`) or as the only content of a JSX comment container among JSX children (`{/* ui-contract-ignore: <reason> */}`), with only white space before the element; each `IgnoreMarker` points at the element after it, and a marker whose own reason is shorter than three characters, or in another form, is a fault (`IGNORE_WITHOUT_REASON`, `IGNORE_MALFORMED`; `IGNORE_WITHOUT_ELEMENT` when no element starts where it points, `IGNORE_NO_CONTROL` when its element, and its subtree for the plain form, holds no control), which makes `contract:coverage` fail and write nothing.

On a branch the pre-commit hook runs the sources check as `contract:check:sources:ahead`, which lets the contract run ahead of the app (see the rule `ahead-mode`); a commit on `main` and `bun run check` take the full check.

Keys it concerns: [`screen.anchors.<name>.holdsInput`](#screen-anchors-name-holdsinput), [`screen.anchors.<name>.marksState`](#screen-anchors-name-marksstate), [`screen.anchors.<name>.fields`](#screen-anchors-name-fields), [`screen.flow.states.<name>.meta.renders`](#screen-flow-states-name-meta-renders).

### The check of the e2e tests

<a id="rule-check-consumers"></a>
`bun run contract:check:consumers` holds the e2e tests under `tests/e2e/src` against the contract. `collectFromTest(text, file)` reads one file with oxc-parser and returns what it names of the contract (`Consumed`, its `ConsumedLiteral`s): the anchor of a `getByTestId('<anchor>')` call; in any string or template literal the `[data-testid="..."]`, `[data-code="..."]`, `[data-state="..."]` and `[data-value="..."]` selectors, in double or single quotes; and the value a `toHaveAttribute('data-<testid|code|state|value>', '<value>')` assertion expects, `.not` included; each argument a plain string or a template literal without `${}`, or a `+` chain of them read as the one string it folds to (a TypeScript wrapper such as `as const` changes nothing), each selector at its line in the source. `scanConsumers(root, tests?)` collects the `.ts` files under `CONSUMER_ROOT` (`tests/e2e/src`) or the folder given.

`compareConsumers(consumed, contract)` wants every anchor known (declared, or derived from a declared base with a suffix it lists in `derived`), every code a code of the contract (a row of the vocabulary or a derived toast code), and every state and value in the vocabulary, or `show` and `hide` once an input renders a `visibility-button`, as the scenarios check resolves its bracket references. A code, a state or a value asserted on an anchor (`ConsumedLiteral.of`) must be one that anchor carries, as `anchorCarries` reads it, as in a scenario: in a selector string, `[data-testid="app-shell.leave-dialog"][data-code="unsaved-input"]`, the attribute selectors of one element pair with its `data-testid`, before or after it (`elementAnchors` in `elements.ts`), attribute selectors written one after another are of one element, and a `:not(`, `:is(` or `:where(` of one compound is that element too (a `data-testid` in a `:not(` naming none); a `:has(`, any other parenthesised argument, or a `:not(` holding a combinator or a list speaks of elements of its own, which pair among themselves, and after its `)` the element before it goes on; a space, a combinator, a list, an expression or other text leads to another element. A `toHaveAttribute('data-code', 'text-required')` pairs with the last `getByTestId('<anchor>')` of the receiver of its `expect(...)` (`assertedAnchor` in `receiver.ts`), `page.getByTestId('organisation.notes.error')`, when every step after it narrows what it found (`first`, `last`, `nth`, `filter`, `and`). A receiver held in a variable or built by a helper pairs with nothing and is held to the contract alone.

A plain string or a template without `${}` is a literal; an anchor built with `${}` or held in a name is a runtime anchor and a problem (`RUNTIME_TEST_ANCHOR`), while a code, a state or a value from an expression is a parameter of the test and passes. A helper such as `notice(page, 'profile-saved')` is not followed, and a `data-id` is data, not contract.

Keys it concerns: [`screen.anchors.<name>.codes`](#screen-anchors-name-codes), [`screen.anchors.<name>.states`](#screen-anchors-name-states), [`screen.anchors.<name>.dataValue`](#screen-anchors-name-datavalue).

### Editions and migrations of a contract

<a id="rule-editions-and-migrations"></a>
A contract is read by the version its `platform.yaml` declares, `editionOf(contract)`, and a rule a version brings applies from that version on, `since(contract, N)` (the contract is at N or later), so a contract at an older version loads and checks as before: a rule of version N starts with `if (!since(contract, N)) return []`. `outdatedProblem(contract)` is a contract older than `CONTRACT_VERSION`, named with the command that moves it, or undefined: `contract:check` passes an older contract and names it in one line.

`migrate(root, { write, migrations?, current? })` runs each `Migration` (`from`, `note`, and `run(files)`, which edits the `MigrationFiles` in place, the platform and the screen files as YAML documents and the vocabulary and the model as text, and returns the work left by hand) from the declared version up to `current`, `CONTRACT_VERSION` by default, from `migrations`, the registry `MIGRATIONS` (one per version, ordered by `from`) by default, setting `version` after each step. It returns the `MigrationReport` (`from`, `to`, and per step its `from`, `note`, the files it changed and the work by hand) and, with `write`, writes the files the steps changed, each document into the text it was read from (`files/write-yaml-document/`): only the nodes a step edited change (a scalar in place, a pair or an item by its lines, a flow collection whose parts changed as a whole), every other byte stays as written. A contract newer than `current`, a platform file without a version, a file that is not YAML and a version no migration reads throw. `parseYamlDocument(text)` is the `yaml` package's `Document` of a text, its comments, the style of every node and its range in the text kept. `packages/ui-contract/MIGRATIONS.md` says what each version changed.

### The business and the meta layer: three kinds of commit

<a id="rule-layers-and-commits"></a>
The contract has a business layer and a meta layer. The business layer is the entry point of every business feature: `contract/domain/` (the screens, the activities, the platform, the model), the scenarios of `tests/e2e/scenarios/`, and the content tables of `contract/vocabulary.md` (the codes, the states, the choice values, the screen names, the roles, the shell's reserved anchors): a row of them travels with the screen file that first uses it. The meta layer is the engine that keeps the contract working: `@workspace/ui-contract` (its schemas, checks, manifest, runtime helpers, and `./machines`, the walk that runs every flow and activity in XState inside `contract:check` and the comparison an app holds its own machines to their flows with), the generated `contract/schemas/` and `contract/REFERENCE.md`, the grammar of `contract/vocabulary.md` (its Grammar section, the kinds, the derived suffixes, the attributes), `contract/README.md`, the rules and skills about the contract, the contract steps of the pre-commit hook, the prose of `COVERAGE.md` and the contract's rows of `docs/agents/context-smoke-test.md`.

An app is one consumer of the engine, a particular case: its code and its tests are the implementation, its runtime of the contract (`window.__perch.ui`, the served manifest) and the specs that hold it to the contract included (the web app's `apps/web/src/platform/machines.spec.ts` with its `APP_MACHINES`, `routes-*.spec.ts`, the e2e spec of the manifest), and so is an example in a meta doc that quotes the app's code, which changes in the implementation commit that changes that code. A meta commit touches an app only where the app consumes what the engine changed (an import path, a call), never what the app does. On a branch a commit holds one of the three kinds, never two. A feature that needs a new facet lands the meta change first (the engine learns it), then commits the business change that uses it. The generated block of `COVERAGE.md` goes with whichever commit changed what it measures, on an area branch: it measures the code too, so the contract trunk leaves it to the implementation that follows the take; `contract:coverage:check` fails a block that is not what `contract:coverage --write` writes, in `bun run check` and in the hook on `main`.

The files are of two kinds. Authored: `contract/vocabulary.md`, `contract/domain/screens/<pathname>.yaml` (`__root.yaml` for the frame), `contract/domain/platform.yaml`, `contract/domain/model.schema.json`, `contract/domain/activities/<id>.yaml`. Generated, never edited by hand: `screen.schema.json`, `platform.schema.json`, `activity.schema.json`, `x-perch.schema.json`, `manifest.schema.json` and `contract/REFERENCE.md` (`bun run contract:schemas`) and the block of `contract/COVERAGE.md`. A word the vocabulary lacks is added to it in the same change. `isBusinessFile(path)` says whether a file is one of the business layer's (a domain file, the vocabulary or a scenario): the only files a commit on the contract trunk may stage.

### Ahead mode: the contract runs ahead of the app on a branch

<a id="rule-ahead-mode"></a>
On a branch the pre-commit hook runs the sources check as `contract:check:sources:ahead`, which lets the contract run ahead of the app: an anchor declared, not rendered yet, passes, and so does a facet of a control's element (`aria-invalid`, `disabled`) not written yet, or still written in its idiom on a field or a control the contract has just stopped calling for it on (an error's code moved off a field, a control's event dropped); the app uses only what the contract declares. A `holdsInput`, a `marksState`, a `maxLength` or a `type` a view writes is refused in ahead mode as in the full check (see the rule `check-sources`): the kit takes them from the generated field facts. A commit on `main` and `bun run check` take the full check, `e2e:coverage` and `contract:coverage:check` included, so nothing lands unrendered, half-built, untested or with its coverage block behind.

The same holds for the app's machines: on a branch the hook runs the unit tests with `CONTRACT_AHEAD=1`, and a machine may lack what its flow added (a state, an event, a guarded branch, an effect, an anchor a state renders) but does nothing the flow does not say, or is still exactly its screen's flow at the branch's merge base with `main` (`CONTRACT_AHEAD_BASE`; during a merge, the base the merge's commit will have, so a merge of `main` compares the app it brings with the flows it brings) or at the commit the branch is at (`CONTRACT_AHEAD_HEAD`, the contract the app was built from: a card's second business commit, taken onto a branch whose app already follows its first, changes a flow the base may not have at all; during a merge, the tip before it, unless the engine the merge brings cannot load that tip's contract for any reason, a key it now requires among them, and then that tip gives no flows to match), so a business commit may change a transition the app has not changed yet. `aheadOf(app, flow)` is the flow as far as a machine that lags it has come (equal to the machine's projection exactly when it does nothing the flow does not say) and `laggingOf(app, flow, base)` the flow at the branch's base when the machine is still exactly that, else `aheadOf`.

The app's routes likewise: a binding may wait for its route, and a route that still reads as its binding did at the merge base (the same screen, the same path) is held to that binding as it was there. A scenario may wait for its test, as the hook leaves `e2e:coverage` out on a branch. The hook names both commits, and the cache of the unit tests keys on them, so a pass never carries into another branch state with the same files. A branch whose merge base with a local `main` cannot be found fails the hook; on `main`, on a detached HEAD and in `bun run check` every machine is its whole flow. A contract at an older version, as the hook's ahead mode reads main's (`CONTRACT_AHEAD_BASE`) with the new engine, loads and checks as before (see the rule `editions-and-migrations`).

A rename or a removal keeps the old name declared until the implementation has switched to the new name or stopped rendering the removed element, then a last business commit drops it. It therefore goes in three commits: the business commit adds the new name and keeps the old one (on the trunk, or with the review stop on an area branch), the implementation switches to it, a last business commit on the area branch drops the old one, approved with the first. The ahead mode cannot drop a name the app still renders.

Keys it concerns: [`screen.anchors.<name>.holdsInput`](#screen-anchors-name-holdsinput), [`screen.anchors.<name>.marksState`](#screen-anchors-name-marksstate).

### The contract trunk holds a commit to the business layer

<a id="rule-trunk-commits"></a>
On `contract/trunk` the pre-commit hook first holds each commit to the business layer (`scripts/contract-trunk.ts`): a commit that stages a file outside `contract/domain/`, `contract/vocabulary.md` and `tests/e2e/scenarios/`, added, changed or deleted, a move counted by both its paths, fails, naming each such file and the branch it belongs on. The vocabulary counts whole, so an edit of its grammar there is the review's to see in the trunk's diff; a merge passes, as it brings `main` in with every layer.

The pre-commit hook first refuses a commit while a hook `lefthook.yml` names is not installed (`scripts/hooks-installed.ts`): lefthook installs the hooks on `bunx lefthook install` (or `bun install`), and by itself on a hook run that notices `lefthook.yml` changed (an edit in the same second as the last install can pass unnoticed), unless told not to (`--no-auto-install`), but a hook whose file went while `lefthook.yml` stayed the same stays missing.

### A take: the form of a business commit on a branch

<a id="rule-take-form"></a>
On any other branch but `main` and `feat/contract-first-pass` (an experiment's aside), the commit-msg hook holds a commit that changes the business layer to a take's form (`scripts/contract-take.ts`): its subject `<type>(contract): <the card's goal>`, the business layer alone, and as its last paragraph `Taken-from: contract/trunk <first>^..<last> <card>`, each commit or run of them oldest first, on the trunk's own line, changing every file it stages; a business commit the rules let the branch write itself (the last one of a rename or a removal, a feature begun there before the trunk, an amendment allowed there) names its cards in `Written-on-branch: <card>` instead. Nothing else there names the `contract` scope, so `git log main..<branch> -E --grep='^[a-z]*\(contract\):'` lists the branch's contract changes (`main..` leaves out the meta rounds `main` brings in) and `--grep='^Taken-from:'` its takes. A plain cherry-pick or rebase runs no hook and a revert of a take fails it.

The check reads the `git commit` that runs the hook from `/proc`, its options parsed as git parses them: an amend is judged as the whole commit it leaves, and the message as git records it, cut at its scissors line under `--verbose` and its comment lines stripped or kept by the cleanup mode; where git's default mode leaves that to whether an editor opened, a message its comment lines change fails, and so does one the comment characters git may pick under `core.commentChar=auto` read differently. Without `/proc` (another system), an amend is judged by what it adds and the message is cut at a scissors line it holds. A take is checked against `main` and `contract/trunk` as the clone knows them, the remote's when it has no branch of its own.

### A landing on main: the squash of one branch

<a id="rule-landing-form"></a>
On `main` the same hook judges every commit as a landing (`scripts/contract-take.ts`), the squash of one branch, against its parent: `main`'s HEAD, or, for an amend, the commit under the one it replaces. A merge into `main` is refused, as `main` takes only squashes; any other commit carries exactly one `Squashed-from: <branch> (<tip>) [<cards>]` trailer as its last paragraph, the cards optional for the hook (the landing procedure still names the card). The branch must exist by its exact ref, `refs/heads/<branch>`, so a revision such as `feat/me~0` names none; the tip, 7 to 40 hex digits, must be that branch's tip or one of its ancestors, and a short id git cannot tell from another commit is refused. The parent must lie under the tip, and the committed tree must equal the tip's tree and differ from the parent's, so a landing is `git merge --squash <branch>` and nothing more or less.

Then the branch's round is judged: the commits of the tip that are neither on the parent nor under the tip of an earlier landing's trailer, merges left out, each against the take rules above as git recorded it, its message and the files it changes. A recorded commit of the round that breaks the take rules stops the squash even when its own hook was skipped (a plain cherry-pick, a `--no-verify` commit). The refusal names each commit that fails and how to rewrite it; the check grants no waiver. `contract/trunk` and an experiment never land, an experiment being split into area branches first. The engine's branch, `feat/contract-first-pass`, is bound by the trailer like any other branch, but its round is not judged, as its meta commits carry the `contract` scope with the engine's own files. `bun run scripts/contract-take.ts --round [<branch>]` prints that verdict for a branch (the checked-out one by default) without writing anything, so it runs before the gates; it ends with `contract take: every commit of <branch> since its last landing (<n>) is in its form` when every commit of the round is.

The check has limits. It sees a commit only where git runs the commit-msg hook for it: a change of `main` that runs none is not seen, a fast-forward, a plain cherry-pick or revert, a rebase, or a commit made with `--no-verify`, the squash's own included. `main` changes only by a squash commit, by procedure, and the check cannot force it. A merge's own resolution is not judged, as a merge of the round is left out. Without `/proc` (another system) an amend is not detected, so the commit is judged against HEAD, and an amend that adds no change to `main` is refused as not changing it. An earlier landing's tip that the clone lacks cannot bound the round: its commits are judged again, and a refusal names those tips, so that the branch which landed them can be fetched. An amend of a landing is made before its merge back into the branch: after it, the replaced landing falls into the round and is judged there.

### The manifest: what it holds, how it is built and where it is served

<a id="rule-manifest"></a>
`bun run contract:manifest` (`ui-contract manifest --out <folder>/ui-manifest`) builds `ui-manifest/manifest.json` from the contract files and the scenarios of `tests/e2e/scenarios/` alone, only after the contract check passes: `emitManifest(root, outDir)` runs the contract check, writes nothing and returns its problems when it finds any, else loads the contract and builds, validates and writes the manifest. `buildManifest` takes only the vocabulary, the screens, the model, the activities, the scenarios, the terms file when the contract writes one, and the product. `writeManifest(root, outDir, manifest)` validates it against `contract/schemas/manifest.schema.json` with Ajv and returns the problems without writing anything, or empties `outDir` (its last segment must be `ui-manifest`, `MANIFEST_DIR`, else a problem) and writes `manifest.json` (two spaces deep, each entry of `ids` on one line, so a search for an anchor shows its facts), `manifest.schema.json` and `model.schema.json` beside it. `productInfo(root)` names the product: the root package's version and the commit checked out, or null.

The web build writes it into `apps/web/dist/ui-manifest/` (`bun run build` runs `ui-contract manifest --out dist/ui-manifest` once Vite is done; the development watcher writes it into `apps/web/dist-dev/ui-manifest/` after every build, and a change of a contract file or of a scenario rebuilds), and both stands serve it at `/ui-manifest/manifest.json`, with `manifest.schema.json` and `model.schema.json` beside it and `Cache-Control: no-store`. `PERCH_UI_MANIFEST=0` on a stand answers 404 there; the default, `1`, serves it.

It is what a tool outside the repository reads (its keys are the Manifest section of this reference): the vocabulary as tables with prose `instructions` for an agent (`instructionsOf`, its fixed texts read from the rules of this reference, the anchors and states taken from the vocabulary and the platform section); `derivedParts`, each derived suffix with the kind of the part it marks, the meaning of the suffix as its `description` and, for a `visibility-button`, its values; `composites`, the vocabulary's table of the platform's composites, whose declarations are under `platform.composites`; `fieldStates`, the table of field states, `{}` when the vocabulary has none; the screens verbatim, each binding with its `example` link and each anchor with its description, the written one (a secret input's with `(type password)` appended once) or else the one the engine says (see the rule `engine-descriptions`); the platform section as written, each value of the platform with its length resolved and each invariant with the actions it limits, derived (`actions`, see the rule `action-outcomes`); the activities verbatim; the scenarios parsed; `routeParams`; the model document, each field's length resolved; from version 6 `contract.defaults`, the default length they took (see the rule `declared-values`); `rules`, the text of each rule of this reference that the manifest or its schema names (wherever a description says "see the rule" and an id in backticks), as the reference writes it (`rulesOf`, taken from the registry the reference is written from), because a tool that reads the manifest alone never sees the reference; a rule only these texts name is not carried; and `ids`.

`ids` lists every declared anchor by name with its kind, its screen and its attributes: `holdsInput` of a field, the `fieldStates` of a field, the `marksState: false` of a field that writes it (the app leaves its border uncoloured; its `fieldStates` still hold), a ranked message's `ranked`, a secret input's `composite`, and `derivedFrom` on the anchors a composite derives (`{ composite }` on its list, `{ composite, item }` on an item), one entry per line, so a search for an anchor in the manifest shows its facts. `fieldStatesOf` gives the states of a field: only an `input`, `textarea` or `select` that holds input has any, `unsaved`, and `saved` too when its binding is a model field; each state must be declared in the vocabulary's "Field states" table, so a vocabulary without it gives none; listed only when not empty.

A contract change hands over `bun run contract:diff main` with the reason for each rename or removal (see the rule `hand-over`).

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.rules`](#manifest-rules).

### The page helper and the report of a settled state

<a id="rule-page-helper"></a>
On a development build, and on a production build made with `PERCH_UI_HOOKS=1` (the e2e stand's), `window.__perch.ui.anchors()` (`PAGE_HELPER`, `__perch.ui`) lists the anchors the page renders now, in document order, each with its kind (the last dash-separated word of its last segment), `data-state`, `data-code`, `data-id`, `data-value` and visibility (`visible` is false when the element or one of its ancestors is hidden), with the page's URL (`RenderedPage`, `RenderedAnchor`). `ANCHOR_ATTRIBUTES` names the attributes (`data-testid`, `data-state`, `data-code`, `data-id`, `data-value`), and `window.__perch` is declared on `Window` for any code that imports the types. Without the helper, the names a page renders are still there:

```js
;[...document.querySelectorAll('[data-testid]')].map((element) => element.dataset.testid)
```

An e2e run exposes the binding `window.__perchSettledState` (`SETTLED_STATE`, declared on `Window`), and such a build reports to it each settled state of a screen's machine, once the page has rendered it: the screen (`app-shell` for the frame), the state value (XState's, `SettledValue`) and the page as the helper reads it then (`SettledState`). The harness holds every report to the manifest the stand serves (`settledProblems`, see the rule `settled-state`), and a test fails at its end when a state it passed through broke it.

### The check of a settled state

<a id="rule-settled-state"></a>
`settledProblems(contract, settled)` is the e2e harness's check of a `SettledState` against the manifest (any `SettledContract`: each screen's `id` and `flow`, and `ids` with each anchor's `screen`, `kind`, `derived`, `roles`, `own`, `dataId`, `states`, `with`, `without` and `derivedFrom`): what the page shows once a screen's machine has settled in a state, held to what that state renders (`meta.renders`, read by `renderedAt`). One line per problem; a screen the contract lacks is one. Rendered means visible to the user: an anchor the state does not render is not visible, absent from the page or hidden on it; one it renders is on the page and visible.

Only the anchors of the screen that render only where listed are held, with their derived parts; the anchors every state renders are not (a message shows when it carries a code, and an error when there is one). Two directions are checked. A visible anchor of the screen that the state does not render is reported (`is visible, but the state does not render it`), whatever the anchor is: this one is never waived, so an anchor with `roles`, `own` or a `dataId` that is visible in a state that does not render it is reported. An anchor the state renders that is missing from the page, or hidden, is reported (`is not on the page, but the state renders it`, `is hidden, but the state renders it`), unless something besides the state decides whether it shows, where the requirement to be present and visible is waived. Data decides for an anchor with `roles` or `own`, an entry of a list (`dataId`), or an anchor nested under one of these. Its own rule decides for a cell (see the rule `empty-cell`), for a list whose entries come from data, any list but a composite's unless its `states` include `loading` or `empty` (see the rule `empty-list`), and for an anchor whose `with` and `without` do not say it shows on the page, or that is rendered in each entry of a list (see the rule `with-without`); so does the rule of an anchor it is nested under or derived from, while that anchor is not visible (the parts of a base that shows follow it). It is waived as well for the parts a page shows on demand: a select's `option`, a composite's `item`, a field's `error-message`, and a paged list's `range`, `previous-link` and `next-link`, which its read decides; the size select shows wherever the list does and is held.

Beside these, an anchor of the screen that says when it shows is held to its entries (`with`, `without`, see the rule `with-without`), and a visible list to its entries (see the rule `empty-list`).

Keys it concerns: [`screen.flow.states.<name>.meta.renders`](#screen-flow-states-name-meta-renders), [`screen.anchors.<name>.roles`](#screen-anchors-name-roles), [`screen.anchors.<name>.own`](#screen-anchors-name-own), [`screen.anchors.<name>.with`](#screen-anchors-name-with), [`screen.anchors.<name>.without`](#screen-anchors-name-without).

### The modules the engine emits for the layers below the contract

<a id="rule-emitted-modules"></a>
The engine emits modules of plain data for the layers below the contract (a server, a database schema, an app's kit) to read what the contract says of a value or a field instead of restating it. `ui-contract emit values --out <file>`, `ui-contract emit fields --out <file>` and `ui-contract emit terms --out <file>` write one from the contract of the working tree, run from the root or any folder under it, into the file the caller names, relative to the folder it runs from: the engine knows no package and writes nowhere by default. A module is one `export const <NAME> = { ... } as const` per constant under a `GENERATED` header, with no import and no function, its entries sorted by their key, one key per line and the same text on every run, so a changed limit is a one-line diff (`valuesModuleOf(contract)`, `fieldFactsModuleOf(contract)`, `termsModuleOf(contract)`). As with the manifest, a contract that fails the contract check is not emitted: its problems are printed and nothing is written. `bun run contract:emit` (`turbo run emit`) runs the `emit` script of every package that keeps one.

`CONTRACT_VALUES` holds every value of `platform.values` by its name and every field of the model as `<entity>.<field>`, each as `resolveValue` gives it: its keywords (`type`, `enum`, `minLength`, `maxLength`, `pattern`, `format`, `minimum`, `maximum`, `readOnly`), each length resolved, so a value that takes the default length carries it and one that writes `maxLength: null` carries none (see the rule `declared-values`). `FIELD_FACTS` holds, by anchor, what the element of a field carries that the contract decides: `maxLength`, the limit of a text field's value (`fieldValueOf`) unless the value is read-only; `type: "email"`, on an input that holds no secret and whose value has `format: email`, a read-only one included (see the rule `email-format`); `marksState: false`, where the field writes it; and `holdsInput: false`, on a field that holds no input (see the rule `field-holds-input`) and on the size select a paged list derives. A field none of these concerns has no entry. `CONTRACT_ROLES` holds every role of the contract by its name, to its name; `CONTRACT_ROUTES` the route path of every binding of a screen: its first by the screen's id, the binding its file is named by, a further one by the id and its path (`<id>@<path>`, `routeKeyOf`). A binding added as a further one moves no key; one added as the first, or a further one made the first, takes the id, and every reader of the id with it. The first binding's path renamed keeps its key, the screen's id, so it reaches every reader; a further binding's path renamed renames its key, a type error at each reader (see the rule `terms`). `CONTRACT_INVARIANTS` holds the numbers of each invariant that declares `values`, by its name: a count by its name, a duration in milliseconds under its name with `Ms` (`window: { minutes: 15 }` is `windowMs: 900000`), so a window kept as `{ limit, windowMs }` is the emitted object (see the rule `action-outcomes`). The kit finds an element's entry by its anchor and gives it what the entry says, so a view writes none of it: the sources check refuses a `maxLength`, a `type` on a text field, a `holdsInput` and a `marksState`, in ahead mode too (see the rule `check-sources`).

An emitted module is implementation: it is committed in the package that reads it and never in a business commit, which stages the business layer alone (see the rules `trunk-commits` and `take-form`), so the implementation that follows a change of the contract emits it again on its area branch. A package that commits one holds it with a spec that asserts the committed text equals `heldModule({ committed, emit, root, env })` of `@workspace/ui-contract/testing`, the one place that says how: the text the engine emits for the contract of the working tree, or, while the contract runs ahead of the app (`CONTRACT_AHEAD`), the committed text itself when it is what the engine emitted for the contract at `CONTRACT_AHEAD_BASE` or `CONTRACT_AHEAD_HEAD` (`contractAt`), as the app's machines may lag their flows (see the rule `ahead-mode`); the entry holds no code a package runs outside its specs. `turbo.json` passes those variables to the unit tests of `@workspace/kernel`, `@workspace/gateway` and `@workspace/database`, as it does to the web app's.

Keys it concerns: [`platform.defaults`](#platform-defaults), [`platform.values`](#platform-values).

### What contract:diff lists of a change

<a id="rule-contract-diff"></a>
`contractAt(ref, root)` reads the contract files, the screen files, the activities and the scenarios at a git ref (`git ls-tree`, `git show`) and loads them, or returns `{ missing: true }` when that commit has no `contract/`; `diffContracts(before, after)` returns the `ContractChanges`, and `bun run contract:diff <ref>` prints them as `formatChanges(changes)` does (see the rule `hand-over`). A contract left undefined is an empty one. The diff lists what both sides write or derive, so a screen written shorter that loads to the same contract shows no change but the descriptions it stops writing.

The lists, in the order of the block, each added and removed and sorted:

- the screens, and the route access lines: who each route serves as `<path> guard <guard>` and `<path> serves <role>`, a route added whole with every line;
- the search parameters of each route, written or derived (the token of a mail link, with its key): a parameter added or removed as `<path> search <name>: <keywords>`, and each keyword it changes as `<path> search <name> <keyword> <before> -> <after>` (`/verify-email search token key "link-invalid" -> "link-expired"`), each side as JSON and `none` where it lacks the keyword. The keywords are the bounds of the value, `default`, `required` (`true`), `key`, `control` and `values` (the control of each enum value); a route added or removed whole lists every parameter, and a description is a description change of its own;
- the anchors, with the anchors renamed (below); the lists that read a platform action as `<anchor> reads platform.<action>`, then each param by name (`q=search:q`, `kind="all"`), then `paged` when it pages: a list moved to another search or to paging shows as its old line removed and its new one added, and a list added whole shows its line;
- on the anchors both sides hold, the derived parts as `<anchor>.<suffix>` (`<input>.visibility-button` for the eye of a password input), the codes as `<anchor> <code>` and the states as `<anchor> <state>`, written or derived, so a constraint added to the model shows as the code it gives and a change of a composite's item state shows on each item it derives, the roles each is shown to as `<anchor> <role>`, an error's fields as `<error> <code> -> <field>`, the fields that hold input as `<anchor> holds input`, the fields that drop their state colour as `<anchor> shows no state colour`, the controls that ask before a destructive action as `<anchor> asks <code>`, the controls that ask before they leave their screen as `<anchor> leaves losing <field>, <field>` (other fields show as the old line removed and the new one added), and the messages that rank their codes as `<anchor> ranks <code> > <code>`, a new order as the old line removed and the new one added, which the codes compared as a set do not show;
- when each anchor shows, the entries of its `with` and `without` (see the rule `with-without`) as `<anchor> with <entry>` and `<anchor> without <entry>`, the entry being the anchor it names, with `: <code> or <code>` when it names codes (as a set, sorted: `device.list.revoke-button without device.list.current-badge`, `app-shell.install-button with app-shell.install-message: install-available`), and, for an entry both sides hold, `<anchor> with <entry> codes <before> -> <after>` when its codes change, `none` on the side that names none. An anchor added or removed whole lists every entry, and an anchor renamed holds its entries, also where an entry names a renamed anchor. The entries are those of the loaded contract;
- the codes of the contract, the composites by name, the entities and their fields as `<entity>.<field>`, the activities by id;
- the actions of `platform.leaving` added and removed, the entries of `platform.unsavedInput` as `<leave>: <how it asks>` (and `question: <dialog>`), what `platform.defaults` declares as `<keyword> <value>` (`maxLength 80`), the values of `platform.values` added, removed and changed (see the rule `declared-values`), and the notices of the platform actions as `<action>.<name>`;
- per activity both sides hold, the transitions added and removed as `<state> on <anchor> -> <target>`;
- per screen both sides hold with a flow on either side, the lines of its flow (see the rule `contract-diff-lines`);
- the platform actions by name, and per platform action either side holds the lines of the action (see the rule `contract-diff-lines`);
- per action of the model either side holds, by `<entity>.<action>`, the reads its `changes` names, as `changes <read>`, added and removed (an action one side lacks names none), listed as `model action lines added in <entity>.<action>` (`model action lines added in profile.updateName: changes accounts.list, changes accounts.read`); an action added or removed whole shows each read it changes;
- last, the descriptions changed (see the rule `description-places`).

An anchor removed and one added are a rename wherever a screen and a kind lost exactly one anchor and gained exactly one: with more on either side the pairing would be a guess, so they stay removed and added. What the anchors both sides hold carry (derived parts, codes, states, roles, error fields, input, colour, questions, ranks, descriptions) is compared under a renamed anchor's new name.

`formatChanges(changes)` prints the block a change hands over: `contract changes: none` when nothing changed, else a summary line (`contract changes: 1 anchor renamed`), then one line per list that is not empty (`anchors renamed: device.list.revoke-button -> device.list.sign-out-button`), the transitions as `transitions added in <activity>`, the lines of a flow as `flow lines added in <screen>`, those of a platform action as `platform action lines added in <action>` and those of an action of the model as `model action lines added in <entity>.<action>`, and last `descriptions changed: <place> <before> -> <after>, ...`.

### The lines of a flow and of a platform action in the diff

<a id="rule-contract-diff-lines"></a>
A screen's flow is listed as `flow lines added in <screen id>` and `flow lines removed in <screen id>`, one line per fact the flow says (`flowLines(flow)`, an internal helper of the hand-over). A state is named by its path (`viewing.form.open`) and descriptions are left out, as they change nothing that runs. A flow one side lacks has no lines, so a flow added or removed whole shows every line; a screen added or removed whole is a change of the screens alone. The lines:

- `initial <state>`, and `<path> initial <state>` for each region;
- `guard <name> takes <code>, <code>`, one per guard by failure code, its codes as written, and `guard <name>: confirms <differs|strays|follows>` or `guard <name>: composite breaks`, one per guard a password pair derives; a guard's sentence is prose and has no line;
- `<path> address <path> <search as JSON, keys sorted>`, those the state's address has;
- `<path> renders <anchor>`, one per anchor of its `meta.renders` (`check-mail renders user.sign-in.resend-button`);
- `<path> entry|exit <effect>`;
- `<path> on <event> -> <target> [<guard>] <effects>`;
- `<path> invoke <action> <input as JSON, keys sorted> [read: false]`;
- `<path> onDone|onError [#<n>] -> <target> [<guard>] [read: false] <effects>`, a branch's `read: false` after its guard (`viewing.member-roles.changing onDone #1 -> idle [ownDemotion] read: false`);
- `<path> final`.

An effect is written `show <anchor>: <codes>`, `clear <anchor>`, `read <anchor>` or `navigate <to> <search as JSON>`. Where order runs the machine (the guarded alternatives of an event or an outcome, the effects of an `entry` or an `exit`), an item of a sequence of more than one carries its position, `#<n>`, and the lines are compared as sets. So a screen file written shorter that loads to the same flow shows no flow line.

A platform action is listed by its name, and its lines as `platform action lines added in <action>` and `platform action lines removed in <action>`, one per fact it declares (`actionLines(action)`, an internal helper): each parameter with its type or the value it refers to, the constraints it writes, each as JSON, and `optional` when it may be left out (`param email: string`, `param login: value email, maxLength 100`, `param kind: string, enum ["a","b"]`), each line of its result (`result: <the line>`, or `result #<n>: <the line>` by its position when there are several, since the first is the plain success; the prose that stays, since it says what the action answers), each code its failure shows with the invariants it refuses for and the condition it answers on (`code invalid-code: "<the condition>"`, `code device-limit: invariant device-limit`, `code rate-limited: invariant failed-sign-ins, address-mails; "<the condition>"`, the condition quoted; a code of the older list, `code rate-limited`), each role that may run it (`role <role>`), its page sizes when it pages (`paged by 10, 25, 50, 25 by default`), each read it changes (`changes organisations.invitations`), its mail (`mail to email language locale link user.verify-email?token`: who it goes to, the language it speaks, the screen its link opens and the search parameter that carries the token) and each notice (`notice password-changed to account language account link user.forgot-password`). An action added or removed whole shows every line, as nothing else in the summary says its mail.

An invariant of the platform is listed by its name, its lines as `invariant lines added in <name>` and `invariant lines removed in <name>`: its rule with its values written in (`rule: <the text>`), the code it refuses with (`refuses <code>`) and each of its values (`value <name>: <value>`). Which actions it limits shows in their own lines, where each names it.

### Written descriptions: their place and their length in the diff

<a id="rule-description-places"></a>
`readDescriptions(root)` reads every description the domain files write, as they write them, never one the engine derives or the loader fills in as it expands a shorter form: the screen files (a screen's own, its route parameters', its anchors', its container `states`, its flow's own, sentence guards, states, regions and transitions with the outcomes of invokes and of a region's button), the platform file (its values, its actions and their parameters, its `events`, its composites' own and their items), the activities (their own, their data, sentence guards, visits and transitions) and the model (its own, each entity's, action's and field's, the subschemas of the document, an entity or a field included), each read as YAML, the model's JSON too, so each has its line. Each is a `WrittenDescription`: its `file`, `line`, JSON path `at`, `kind` (the field it is, a `DescriptionKind`), `place` and `text`. The `Contract` carries them as `descriptions`, read by `loadContract`, so the contract at a git ref has those its files write there.

The place names a description alike at any ref, by what holds it, never by the depth of its path: `app-shell.theme-select`, `user.profile` (the screen's own), `user.profile state loading`, `user.profile flow`, `user.profile flow editing.save`, `user.delete-account flow deleting onError #2` (the second of its guarded alternatives), `user.profile guard nameChanged`, `user.sign-in binding /sign-in search email`, `platform action sign-up param email`, `platform event started`, `platform composite password-rules`, `model`, `model entity profile`, `model action profile.updateName`, `model field profile.name`, `activity session state profile on user.profile.sign-out-button`.

`descriptionChanges(before, after, renamed)` lists, sorted by place, each description that one side adds, removes or changes, a `DescriptionChange { place, before?, after? }` with its length in characters on each side it has, a renamed anchor's under its new name: `app-shell.theme-select 191 -> 340` (a shrink alike, `340 -> 191`), `new -> 120` for one added, `340 -> gone` for one removed, and the same length on both sides for a text changed at its length. It prints last, as `descriptions changed: <place> <before> -> <after>, ...`, so a description that grows clause by clause shows on review even where nothing that runs changed. Each description keeps the budget of its field (see the rule `check-files`).

### Handing over a change

<a id="rule-hand-over"></a>
A change to the contract goes with the output of `bun run contract:diff main` in its description (the screens, anchors, codes, entities, fields and activities added, removed or renamed, the composites added or removed, the derived parts, the codes, the states, the roles and the error fields the anchors both sides hold gain or lose, the messages among them that rank their codes, with their order, when each anchor shows by its `with` and `without` entries, who each route serves, the search parameters of each route, the platform action each list reads with its params and its paging, the platform actions added or removed and the notices they declare, the transitions added or removed in each activity both sides hold, the lines of the flow added or removed in each screen both sides hold with a flow, the lines of each platform action added, removed or changed, the reads each action of the model comes to change or stops changing, and each written description added, removed or changed with its length before and after), and below it, by hand, the reason for each rename or removal, since those break whoever reads the old name:

```text
<the output of bun run contract:diff main>

Why:
- <each anchor, code, screen, entity, field, activity or transition renamed or removed>: <the reason>
Scenarios and tests changed with it: <scenario ids>
```

For example, renaming the revoke button of the devices page:

```text
contract changes: 1 anchor renamed
anchors renamed: device.list.revoke-button -> device.list.sign-out-button

Why:
- device.list.revoke-button: the button signs the device out, and the anchor now says so
Scenarios and tests changed with it: device/base/my-devices
```

The tests are in this repository, so the same change updates them, and the consumers check fails until it does. This is the delivery of a finished change; the review before the code is the owner's process of work.

### Coverage: how far the contract reaches

<a id="rule-coverage"></a>
`bun run contract:coverage` (`ui-contract coverage [--write | --check] [--json] [--unmarked] [--descriptions]`) prints how far the contract reaches, and `--write` puts the same block into `COVERAGE.md`, between the markers `<!-- coverage:start -->` and `<!-- coverage:end -->` (`COVERAGE_START`, `COVERAGE_END`): the text outside them is written by hand, and `replaceCoverageBlock(note, block)` throws when the note lacks one marker of each, in that order. It is a report, not a gate: no measure fails a commit. The block stays current, though: `bun run contract:coverage:check` (`ui-contract coverage --check`, `coverageDrift(note, block)`) fails when the block in `COVERAGE.md` is not what `--write` would write, naming that command at the line of its start marker, and says so in one line when it is. It runs in `bun run check` and in the pre-commit hook on a commit on `main`; on a branch, the contract trunk included, the hook leaves it out, so a block left behind fails `bun run check` before a landing, and the squash on `main`.

1. **The model**, the measure of completeness (`modelCoverage`): per entity of `model.schema.json`, each field and each action with the anchors whose `binding` (one or a list) names it, or `unbound`. A field may be internal (an id, a creation time), so an unbound one is a question, not an error. Then per activity (`activityCoverage`), the scenarios that name it in their `activity` and the transitions they walk, one per branch of each `on`: a scenario is read in the order of its file (`where`, the steps and the expected results, each text's references left to right), with an address per device, a text that opens with `On device X` or `Device X:` speaking for device X, any other for the scenario's only device or for the device last named (`A` at first); a route moves its device to that address, and an anchor named in a step walks the transition on it from every state at its device's address, or from the initial state while that device has named no route. The totals line adds `activities n, transitions walked x/y`.
2. **The controls**, the secondary measure (`countControls(text, file)`): per feature module of the web app (`FEATURES_DIR`) and for the UI kit (`UI_KIT_DIR`, `UI_KIT_MODULE`), how many controls carry an anchor, which are left unmarked and which are declared off the coverage. A control is a JSX element named like one (`CONTROL_TAGS`) or any element with an `onClick`; it is marked when it carries a `data-testid`, `testId` or `*TestId` prop, or sits inside a `<label>`; an element inside a marked control without an action of its own is a part of that control and is not counted, and neither is one that passes on the props it receives. `--unmarked` lists the unmarked and the ignored ones as `file:line: <element>`. The count is an indicator read from the source text, not a rule of the contract.

The block also lists, per screen, its anchors and the scenarios that reach it. Its totals line reads `fields bound x/y, actions bound x/y`, then `activities n, transitions walked x/y` when the contract has any, with a table of them after the model, before the controls marked and ignored. After the totals, a note names each composite the platform declares and no screen opts into (`Note: composite <name> is declared and used by no screen.`, `unusedComposites`), which fails nothing.

A control that is not a test target is declared off the coverage by a marker right before its element, with only white space between, and its reason on the marker line itself, at least three characters long (`// ui-contract-ignore-self: no screen renders a sheet yet; the first one anchors it`). In code it is a line comment, `// ui-contract-ignore: <reason>`; among JSX children it is the only content of a comment container, `{/* ui-contract-ignore: <reason> */}`. `ui-contract-ignore` covers the element and everything inside it, `ui-contract-ignore-self` the element alone. A marker without a reason, in another form, not right before an element, or that ignores no control (its element, and for `ui-contract-ignore` everything inside it, holds none) makes the command fail and write nothing (see the rule `check-sources`).

`bun run contract:coverage --descriptions` (`listDescriptions(contract)`) lists as `<anchor>: <description>` every written description equal to the one the engine says itself, for the developer to drop; an anchor a composite derives is never listed (see the rule `engine-descriptions`).

### Language

<a id="rule-language"></a>
The page renders `en` on the stands, and its wording is not part of the contract. Assert `data-code` (`contract.codes`), `data-state`, `data-id` and `data-value`, never text; the only text a test reads is text it entered itself.

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.language`](#manifest-contract-instructions-language).

### Sessions and platform actions

<a id="rule-session"></a>
Every scenario starts in a fresh browser context with nobody signed in (`guest`). What a platform action does is read from `platform.actions.<name>`, the platform file: its `description`, its `result` (what it answers: one outcome, or outcome lines, the plain success first and each other outcome under its condition), its `codes` (each code a failure shows, with the condition it answers on and the invariants of `platform.invariants` it refuses for, each named once, whose `rule` is said once there and whose `actions` are the actions it limits), its `changes` (written on every action, `[]` where it changes none: the reads whose answer, for the organisation, the account and the params it ran with, differs once it succeeds, platform actions a list, a section or a table takes as its `source`) and, when it sends mails, its `mail` and its `notices`; never assume it from the action's name. One account per browser: a second account, or a second device of the same one, needs a browser context of its own.

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.session`](#manifest-contract-instructions-session).

### Reading the screens

<a id="rule-screen-map"></a>
`screens` lists every screen once. Its `id` is the screen name its anchors start with; its `title` is what a reader calls it and its `description` what it is for. `bindings` are its routes in the router's format (TanStack): `path` with static segments, `$param`, `{-$param}` (optional) and `$` (splat); `guard` (`guest` or `authenticated`: where an account the route is not for is sent, a guest to sign-in or a signed-in account to the profile); `roles` (the roles it serves: the router serves it only to an account that holds one, after its `guard` and before it reads the search, and opens the screen of the binding at `/$`, the not-found screen, at the same address for any other); `params` and `search` as flat JSON Schemas; `history` (`push` or `replace`); `retain` (search parameters kept across navigations); `example`, a deep link to the route with fake data, written as the router writes a link: each path parameter at its first `examples` value, every search key at its `default`, else its first `enum` value or example (the app's own links leave out a value equal to its default and reach the same state). `frame: true` marks the frame around every screen, which has no route. Every binding `path` is a TanStack Router path in the router's own syntax, `params` are its path parameters and `search` its search parameters, and a route is a state address: the same path with the same search reaches the same state after a reload. `entry` lists the anchors present once the screen is open; some are alternatives by state, so wait for the one the state shows. `anchors` maps each declared anchor to its `kind`, `binding`, `codes` (its `data-code` values), `fields` (an error's codes, each with the field it concerns, which the error shows under and marks invalid; every other code shows beside the controls of its part, rule `error-placement`), `dataId` (what its `data-id` holds), `dataValue` (it or its options carry a `data-value` of `contract.choiceValues`), `states` (its `data-state` values), `derived` (the suffixes it renders as `<anchor>.<suffix>`), `roles` (who sees it, among the roles its screen's bindings serve; hidden wherever the roles the device holds now do not admit it, in every state of the flow, a final one included; absent: every role of the screen), `own` (shown only on the entries the member owns), `with` and `without` (it shows only while each `with` entry shows and no `without` entry does; an entry is an anchor of its screen, or `{ "<anchor>": [<code>, …] }`, that anchor while it shows one of these codes; an anchor rendered in each entry of a list names anchors of the same entry), `ranked` (a message whose `codes` are in rank order: of the codes raised before it shows, it shows the first, and the others go with it; a `clear` of it, in any flow, takes away every code raised before it), `secret` (a field that holds a secret, a password: it never leaves the page but in the request of the action that takes it, never into an address and never filled from one) and `source` (where a list's entries come from: a platform action, which the list reads each time it starts to render after it did not, and, while it renders, each time the organisation or account it reads for becomes another or a search parameter it reads does, and where a flow says `read`, with `params` (each a search parameter of the route, `{ "search": "<name>" }`, or a constant) and `paged: true` (one page of the action's entries: the page and the size the route's search parameters `page` and `size` name; the list derives `range`, whose `data-from`, `data-to` and `data-total` say which entries the page shows of how many, `previous-link` and `next-link`, each only where there is such a page, and `size-select`, whose options carry the action's `paged.sizes` and choose the route's `size`, which writes no `x-perch`; the select holds no input, which the field facts carry for the kit; a paged list has at most 3 segments, as its options add two to its name); or an entity of the model, which it follows as the stored data changes). A screen's `states` say what each container state shows, and its `wireframe` is the default state as a Playwright aria snapshot. A screen's `flow` is an XState v5 machine config: `initial`, `states` with `on` (an event is the user acting on that anchor, typing included for a field, a platform event of `platform.events`, or a data event `model.<entity>.<field>`: the stored value of a field the screen shows changed, here or on another device), `target`, `guard` (a name of `meta.guards`), `actions` (effects `show`, `clear`, `read` and `navigate`: `clear` empties a field of the screen, a secret one included, so it holds no input any more, as if nothing was typed, and makes an anchor that carries codes show none until one is shown again; `read` makes a list of the screen whose `source` is a platform action read that action again and show its entries once the read is in, the app taking them from the answer of the action that just ran when it returns them; such a list also reads it each time it starts to render after it did not and, while it renders, each time the organisation or account it reads for becomes another or a search parameter it reads does, and a navigate that sets a search parameter a paged list reads says `page: 1`; one whose `source` is an entity follows the stored data and is never read; a navigate's `search` gives each key a literal, `{ "anchor": "<field>" }`, the value that field of the screen holds when the app moves, or `{ "item": "<item>" }`, the `data-id` of the entry of that item or row whose control the user acted on, a control (a button, a link, a tab or a field) in the item's part (`<part>.<name>`) on whose event the navigate runs; an item or a row as a value of `params` or `input` gives the `data-id` of the entry whose control acts, likewise), `invoke` (a model or platform action with its `input`, whose values are as a binding's `params`, `onDone`, `onError`; `read: false` on an invoke says nothing reads after its action, which changes nothing a list of the screen shows, and `read: false` on one branch of its `onDone` or `onError` says nothing reads after that outcome, where another branch reads; never both, never beside a `read` of the same branch, never on the transition of an event; after an invoke that does not say `read: false`, every branch of its `onDone` reads each list of the screen with a platform source that its action may change, or says `read: false`: a list one of whose entries its `input` takes, one whose source's `params` take a value its `input` takes, and one whose source its action's `changes` names; a failure holds no read, so an `onError` reads where it says), `type: final`, and `meta.address` on a state a route reaches. A state of the root may be `type: parallel`: its `states` are regions, the screen's independent areas, all active at once, each with its `initial` and its plain states; a target of the root may name a region's state as `<parallel>.<region>.<state>` (the other regions start at their `initial`), a target in a region names a state of that region or, by the flow's `id` (a one-segment name the flow declares, which the app gives its machine), a state of the root as `#<flow id>.<state>`, which leaves the parallel state and enters that state afresh, and within a parallel state an event is handled by one region or by the state's own `on`, never by two. A state's `meta.renders` names anchors of the screen that only some states render: an anchor no state of the flow lists, neither itself nor one it is under, is rendered in every state, one listed, itself or through one it is under, only in the states that list it or that one, a state of a region rendering what its parallel state lists too; the anchors nested under a listed one (`<anchor>.<rest>`, such as the items of a list) are rendered with it, or where they are listed themselves. Rendered means visible to the user, as far as the state decides: an anchor a state renders shows unless a condition of its own, of an anchor it is under or of its base if it is a derived part (`roles` or `own`, an entry's data, `with` and `without`) keeps it hidden, and one it does not render is absent from the page or hidden on it. A guard of `meta.guards` is a sentence saying when it holds; `{ "codes": [<code>, …] }`, which guards only a branch of an invoke's `onError` and holds when its action failed with one of those codes; or one the contract derives for a password pair, whose confirmation, a secret input, names in `confirms` the input it repeats: `{ "confirms": "differs" }`, the confirmation is not the same as the confirmed input; `{ "confirms": "strays" }`, the confirmation is no longer how the confirmed input begins: a character differs, or it is longer; `{ "confirms": "follows" }`, the confirmation is how the confirmed input begins, all of it or a part, or is empty; `{ "composite": "breaks" }`, the confirmed input breaks one of the rules of its composite's list. The composite's `items` are the judge: the input breaks the rules while one of them is not met. Carry it into `setup({ guards, actions, actors }).createMachine(flow)` unchanged, implementing the guards it names, the four effects and an actor per `invoke.src`; an invoke's `read: false`, or a branch's, is a statement of the contract, nothing the machine runs. A control whose anchor is an event of the flow (a button, a select, a switch) is enabled exactly while a state the machine is in handles that event (`snapshot.can`), and disabled otherwise: a Save disabled while it saves is the flow saying so, never a flag of its own. A `visibility-button` (`<input>.visibility-button`, the eye of a secret input, in `contract.derivedParts`) shows what the input holds as typed, or hides it again: presentation only, it is never an event of a flow and always enabled, and its `data-value` names what a click does, `show` or `hide`. `ids` holds the same anchors flat, each with its screen. On a development or e2e build, `window.__perch.ui.anchors()` lists the anchors the page renders now, with their attributes and visibility. `composite` on a secret input names the platform composite it opts into (`platform.composites.<name>`): the screen's `anchors` then hold its list and items, each marked `derivedFrom` with the composite and, for an item, its name; they are declared anchors like any other.

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.screenMap`](#manifest-contract-instructions-screenmap).

### Field states

<a id="rule-field-states"></a>
A field carries `data-state` `unsaved` while it holds input no save has taken; one whose `fieldStates` lists `saved` carries `saved` once a save stored what it shows, while it stays on the screen; a field carries no `data-state` otherwise.

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.screenMap`](#manifest-contract-instructions-screenmap).

### Bindings

<a id="rule-bindings"></a>
`{ "model": "<entity>.<field>" }` shows or edits `model.$defs.<entity>.properties.<field>`. `{ "action": "<entity>.<action>" }` runs an action of `model.$defs.<entity>.x-perch.actions`; `{ "action": "platform.<name>" }` runs `platform.actions.<name>`. Its `params` name, per parameter, the anchor whose value it takes, a constant `{ "value": ... }`, or `{ "search": "<name>" }`, the value of that search parameter of the route the screen was opened at. An action of `platform.actions` that sends a mail declares it under `mail`: `to` (the parameter whose value it goes to, or `account`: the email of the account the action acts on, the signed-in account for an action on its own account, else the one its parameters name, whoever is signed in), `language` (a parameter holding a language, `account`, `organisation`: the organisation the action runs in, or `recipient`: the account whose email the mail goes to, when one has it; or a list of these, the first that has a language speaking, `[ recipient, organisation ]` for an invitation; one source or a list, the language of a mail ends with a source that always has one, so never with `recipient`) and `link` (the `screen` its link opens and the `search` parameter carrying its token); a scenario names it `[mail:<action>]`. Its `notices` are mails that only tell, with no token, each with its `name`, `to` (a parameter, or `account`: the email of the account the action acts on, as it was before the action changed anything), `language` and, optionally, a plain `link` to a `screen` whose routes need no parameter; a scenario names one `[notice:<action>.<name>]`. `{ "route": "/path" }` navigates to the path of a binding of some screen. `{ "search": "<name>" }` on a field starts it with the value of that search parameter of the route the screen was opened at and gives it that value again each time the parameter becomes another, which the user may change; it may sit in a list beside the action the field gives its value to. A list of bindings runs in its order, or one of them by the mode of the screen; the `flow` says which. An anchor with `asks` asks the frame's confirmation (`asks.dialog`, with `asks.code`) before its action runs: the app's platform gate opens it, confirm runs the action and cancel does not; a screen builds no dialog of its own for it. An anchor with `leaves` is a control whose flow moves to another screen: the app's platform gate asks the frame's leave question (`leaves.dialog`) before its event while one of the fields of `leaves.loses` holds unsaved input, leave goes on and stay does not; the fields the move carries in its search are not among them. A button's action `params` are the union of the params the fields of its part pass for that action, a param the screen writes on the button winning; an error's `codes`, unless the screen writes them, are the failures of the actions bound beside it and invoked against it by the flow, plus the codes the flow shows on it, `failed` last; an invoke without `onError` whose action fails with codes clears its part's error on entry and, back in the one state that entered it on a control, shows the failure there (a start state, one several states enter, or one a platform or data event enters, writes its `onError`), and an `onError` written as its target alone still shows the failure on that error, on the screen's error when no control enters and the input names no field.

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.bindings`](#manifest-contract-instructions-bindings).

### Reading the model

<a id="rule-model"></a>
`model` is the data model: a JSON Schema 2020-12 document with one `$defs` entry per entity. An entity's `x-perch` says its `table`, its `region` (`private`: the account's own data; `tenant`: the current organisation's), its `id` prefix, its `owner` field, `can` (per role, the actions allowed on any entry or only on its own), its `actions` with their parameters and their `changes` (written on every action, as a platform action writes it), and `unique` and `index`. A field's `x-perch` says whether it is `readonly`, the entity a `record` points at, and its `crdt`: how concurrent edits merge (`text`, `list`, `counter`, or `none` for the last writer). `terms`, when present, says what each of the platform's nouns and each role means, the words the contract never uses for it, and its name in storage where that differs (`storage`).

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.model`](#manifest-contract-instructions-model).

### Reading the activities

<a id="rule-activities"></a>
`activities` are the activities across screens, one file each in `contract/domain/activities/<id>.yaml`, carried here verbatim: which screens form one task, in which order, and what the task carries from one screen to the next. An activity has an `id`, a `title`, a `description` (what it is for), the `roles` that take part and `data` (what it carries, by name, each with a `description` and, when it is a field of the model, its `model` as `<entity>.<field>`). The rest is an XState v5 machine config in the subset of the flows, without effects or `invoke`: `initial`, `meta.guards` and `states`, where a state is a visit to a screen: `meta.screen` names the screen (one screen may be visited by several states), `meta.address` the `path` of a binding of that screen (the activity is where its address is), and `type: final` ends the activity. A state's `on` maps an anchor of its screen or of the frame to where acting on it leads: `target` (the next visit), `guard` and `description`. The activity says where a task leads and the screens do the work: after that anchor, the flow of its screen navigates to the target's address, or the anchor is a link to it (`binding.route`). Carry each into `setup({ guards }).createMachine(activity)` unchanged, as the flows, implementing the guards it names; a scenario that walks an activity names it in its `activity`.

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.activities`](#manifest-contract-instructions-activities).

### Reading the scenarios

<a id="rule-scenarios"></a>
`scenarios` are the end-to-end scenarios in the spec-instruction shape, authored as Markdown files in the e2e tree (`tests/e2e/scenarios/<entity>/<layer>/[<area>/]<slug>.md`: a YAML front matter with `tags`, an optional `activity`, `devices` and `data`, then the sections `# <title>`, `Where:`, `## Scenario:`, `### Steps` and `### Expected result`) and carried here parsed. Their fields: `id` (the file's path under `tests/e2e/scenarios/` without `.md`, `<entity>/<layer>/[<area>/]<slug>` with the layer one of `base`, `flows`, `features`; the test is `tests/e2e/src/<id>.e2e.spec.ts`), `title`, `tags` (`smoke` or `full`), `activity` (the id of the activity of `activities` it walks, when it walks one), `devices` (a letter per browser with the role it reaches by the end; every device starts as `guest`), `where`, `data` (`generate` or a fixed value), `scenario`, `steps` and `expected`. Brackets in their text reference the contract: `[<anchor>]` (in `ids`, or derived from one with a suffix of its `derived`), `[/path]` (a route of `screens`), `[code:<code>]`, `[state:<state>]`, `[value:<value>]`, `[mail:<action>]` (a platform action that declares the mail it sends) and `[notice:<action>.<name>]` (a notice of that name the platform action declares). They are the contract's feedback loop: a scenario that cannot be written from the contract alone (an anchor, a code, a state, a route or a role it would need) names a gap to fix in the contract files, never one to work around in a test.

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.scenarios`](#manifest-contract-instructions-scenarios).

### Contract gaps

<a id="rule-contract-gaps"></a>
When the contract cannot say what you need (a state, a region, an event, a value, a route, a role, a code, a shape), stop at that part: build what the contract covers and report a contract gap with your result, as four lines: `Contract gap:` what is needed; `Facet:` which part of the contract cannot say it (the model, a screen file, the platform, the flow subset, an activity, the scenario grammar, a check); `Emulation refused:` the workaround you did not build; `Proposal:` the change to the contract and its cost. Never hold it in the app or in a test instead: state in a component for a flow state or a region the contract lacks, a value or a route it does not carry, an anchor or a code born in the code or in a test. An emulation is a fork of the model that no check sees and the next build from this manifest loses; the contract is changed first. The one transient state a component holds is whether a password input shows what it holds, which the `visibility-button` the contract declares on it toggles.

Keys it concerns: [`manifest.contract.instructions.gaps`](#manifest-contract-instructions-gaps).
